| FR-061 | Per-Business domain visibility: `resolveViewer()` resolves which domains a principal may see **per Business**, not once per principal. The viewer gains `domainsByBusinessId` (per-Business allow-list; absent or `[]` denies), and `domainsForBusiness(viewer, businessId)` is the only way a Business-scoped consumer may ask the question. The existing flat `visibleDomains` is retained and additively redefined as the union across visible Businesses — "may this principal see this domain *anywhere*" — and is never an authorization input for a Business-scoped decision. | FR-065 | Import target authorization: the FR-012 pipeline authorizes the Workspace it is about to write to, instead of accepting whichever `workspaceId` the request body names. All three intake routes (`/api/import/dry-run`, `/api/import/commit`, `/api/import/xlsx`) resolve a viewer, and the target Workspace is resolved **before** validation so an unauthorized target is refused without the caller learning anything about the plan. (a) A **Business-scoped** Workspace requires `ownsBusiness(viewer, workspace.businessId)` — the same predicate the other write paths use, not a second reading of it; a caller who may see the Business but does not own it is refused. (b) A Workspace **above Business** (`scopeType` PORTFOLIO or TENANT) is refused with an explicit reason stating that no authority above Business is declared — this requirement deliberately does **not** invent one. Enabling such an import requires a prior FR that makes portfolio/tenant authority *holdable*: a viewer-contract change in the manner of FR-061, since today no principal can hold it. The dry run is authorized identically to the commit — a read-only preview of another scope's contents is the leak the commit guard would otherwise still allow. | `app/api/import/commit/route.js`, `app/api/import/dry-run/route.js`, `app/api/import/xlsx/route.js`, `modules/project-manager/import/import-authorization.js`, `modules/project-manager/import/plan-import-service.js` | `e2e/smoke.spec.js`, `integration/external-ref-import.test.js`, `integration/fr075-restore-authorization.test.js`, `integration/import-target-authorization.test.js`, `integration/plan-import-scope.test.js`, `integration/plan-import.test.js`, `integration/project-business-binding.test.js`, `integration/xlsx-intake.test.js`, `unit/import-authorization.test.js` | ✅ |

| FR-073 | Repository ownership: a `Repository` is owned by exactly one Business (`Repository.businessId`), which is the decision FR-072 recorded as missing for `/api/repositories` and `/api/repositories/[id]`. Business rather than Tenant or Portfolio is forced, not chosen: the viewer contract carries only Business-keyed grants, so no principal can hold authority above Business (SDD-037) and a higher-scoped Repository would be ungovernable. (a) Creating a Repository requires `ownsBusiness(viewer, businessId)`, and the Business is required at the input boundary so every new Repository is governed. (b) Updating one requires the same authority over its owning Business; an unowned Repository answers exactly as a nonexistent one. (c) Linking a Repository to a Project requires authority over **both** the Project's governing Business and the Repository's Business — the same fail-closed composition FR-072 applies to a Dependency edge. (d) `Repository.businessId` is nullable only for rows predating this requirement; such a Repository is governed by nobody, is refused for every principal with the missing owner named, and is invisible to every reader until backfilled (`scripts/backfill-repository-business.mjs`, which infers an owner only where a Repository's Project links agree and reports the rest rather than guessing). (e) Listing Repositories is scoped by `seesBusiness`, closing a cross-tenant read that previously returned every Repository in the installation with its project links attached. | `prisma/seed.js`, `app/(pm)/repositories/page.jsx`, `app/api/repositories/[id]/route.js`, `app/api/repositories/route.js`, `lib/validation/entities.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/project-authorization.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/repository-service.js` | `e2e/fr041-business-first.spec.js`, `e2e/smoke.spec.js`, `integration/crm-conversation-analysis.test.js`, `integration/fr072-file-asset-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr072-files-migrate-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr072-refusal-disclosure.test.js`, `integration/fr072-repository-link-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr073-repository-scope.test.js`, `integration/project-authorization.test.js`, `integration/project-core.test.js`, `integration/project-inventory.test.js`, `unit/repositories-page-ui-contract.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-074 | Scope-creation authorization: every creator behind `POST /api/scope` resolves a viewer and is authorized at the scope it actually writes, in three tiers. (a) **Business scope** — `branch` and a BUSINESS-scoped `workspace` require `ownsBusiness(viewer, businessId)`. (b) **Tenant scope** — creating a `business` inside an existing Tenant, or a TENANT-scoped `workspace`, requires `ownsTenant(viewer, tenantId)`, surfaced on the viewer contract as `ownedTenantIds` from the tenant-wide OWNER Membership that already existed in the data and was previously only expanded into `ownedBusinessIds`. This names a held row; it grants nobody anything new, and is deliberately not satisfied by owning every Business in a Tenant. (c) **Self-service provisioning** — `businessInGroup` creates a new Tenant + Business + Workspace for any authenticated principal, because nothing exists to own beforehand, and **binds the creator as OWNER in the same transaction**. Without that binding the caller would provision scope they cannot subsequently write to, which is the defect this clause fixes as well as authorizes. `portfolio`, `tenant`, `legalEntity` and a PORTFOLIO-scoped `workspace` are installation primitives above any Tenant and require FR-075 operator authority. Self-service is never unauthenticated: the viewer is resolved, the write is attributed, and an audit event is recorded. | `app/api/scope/route.js`, `modules/identity/resolve-viewer.js`, `modules/identity/viewer-authority.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/scope-service.js` | `integration/adaptive-shell.test.js`, `integration/fr072-workspace-mutation-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr074-scope-creation-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr106-api-access-key-listing.test.js`, `integration/scope-and-isolation.test.js`, `integration/workspace-onboarding-flow.test.js`, `unit/api-access-auth.test.js`, `unit/authorization-seam-routes.test.js`, `unit/fr061-per-business-domain-visibility.test.js`, `unit/fr076-product-owner-business-assignment.test.js`, `unit/platform-users-view.test.js`, `unit/viewer-authority.test.js`, `unit/viewer-gate.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-075 | Installation-operator authority: an installation-wide operation is authorized by a named capability, `isOperator`, carried on the viewer contract and true only for a server-held platform grant (FR-031, on a trusted session per SEC-008), and false for every ordinary authenticated principal however much of the installation they own. It governs `POST /api/backup/import` — both the preview and the restore, since a preview of every table across every tenant is the same disclosure the restore guard would otherwise still permit — and the scope primitives above any Tenant (FR-074). This is a distinct *scope* of authority rather than a larger amount of Business ownership: a restore replaces Portfolios, Tenants, identities and audit rows, so no composition of `ownsBusiness` can express it, which is why the route was unrepayable until the capability was named. `isPlatform` remains visibility-only and is never read as authority (RCA 2026-08-16). | `app/(pm)/settings/page.jsx`, | FR-081 | Raw external ingestion boundary: every acquisition channel (webhook, pull, file, manual) converges on one normalized ingestion envelope carrying tenant, Business, connection, provider, lane, entity type, external id, source type and schema version, and a channel is added as an adapter rather than a second raw-write path. (a) Ingestion identity is `sha256(tenantId, connectionId, entityType, externalId, payloadHash)` over a canonically serialized payload, so a re-delivered event resolves to `UNCHANGED` instead of a duplicate row; the external id contributes to that identity and is never itself a key (BR-002). (b) Raw records are read and written only through a repository bound to one tenant/connection scope, which refuses a row outside it rather than filtering afterwards (SEC-001), and a referenced Business or IngestionRun must itself resolve inside that scope. (c) Raw ingestion persists the source payload verbatim and never writes domain truth: translation into business entities is a separate later path, so a failed translation cannot corrupt the evidence it was derived from. (d) A run records its own counts and terminal state, and a failure is preserved as a DeadLetterRecord naming the failing stage and owner rather than being retried silently. This requirement declares the ingestion substrate only; no scheduler, no translation ACL and no reader surface is in scope. | `app/api/agent/line-webhook/route.js`, `modules/integration/adapters/marketplace-listing-adapter.js`, `modules/integration/adapters/retail-price-adapter.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/backup-service.js`, `platform/integrations/core/contracts.js`, `platform/integrations/core/idempotency.js`, `platform/integrations/core/integration-registry.js`, `platform/integrations/core/raw-ingest-service.js`, `platform/integrations/core/raw-record-redaction.js`, `platform/integrations/core/raw-record-repository.js`, `platform/integrations/providers/line/line-oa-evidence.js`, `platform/integrations/providers/line/line-oa-webhook.js` | `integration/agent-webhook-route.test.js`, `integration/backup.test.js`, `integration/crm-conversation-analysis.test.js`, `integration/crm-customer-erasure.test.js`, `integration/fr075-restore-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr109-knowledge-ingestion-executor.test.js`, `integration/line-oa-connection-health.test.js`, `integration/line-oa-evidence-convergence.test.js`, `integration/line-server-backup.test.js`, `integration/market-intelligence-translation-run.test.js`, `integration/marketing-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-content-backup.test.js`, `integration/platform/integration-persistence.test.js`, `integration/server-line-webhook.test.js`, `unit/fr045-backup-contract.test.js`, `unit/fr079-runtime-cutover.test.js`, `unit/integration/marketplace-listing-adapter.test.js`, `unit/integration/retail-price-adapter.test.js`, `unit/platform/integration-contracts.test.js`, `unit/platform/line-oa-webhook.test.js`, `unit/platform/raw-ingest-service.test.js`, `unit/platform/raw-record-repository-read.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-082 | Structure editing by direct manipulation: the project Structure Plan becomes editable in place — a `+` affordance on a node adds a child of the type the hierarchy allows at that level, and a node is reparented by drag. An invalid drop target is refused *during* the drag rather than accepted and explained afterwards. Layout stays derived; no node position is persisted (ADR-035 D3). Every drag ships with its single-pointer equivalent in the same change (`Move to…`), because NFR-008 binds WCAG 2.2 AA and SC 2.5.7 *Dragging Movements* is AA. | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
| FR-083 | Edge creation by direct manipulation: the project Dependency Map becomes the canvas where edges are made. Dragging from a source node's handle to a target node proposes an edge, and **the contract dialog that follows is not skippable** — cancelling it cancels the edge (BR-017). Self-edges and cycles are refused by calling the existing FR-007 rule, never a second implementation, and a refusal renders at the attempted edge with its reason. The canvas keeps its derived layout and its accessible edge-list twin, which is what makes the keyboard equivalent (select source → `Connect to…` → pick target) affordable rather than a second interaction model. | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
| FR-084 | Handoff Contract on a dependency edge: every `Dependency` carries a declared contract stating the **deliverable** the predecessor owes and the **acceptance** condition that says the debt is paid. Acceptance either references an existing `Gate` — whose status is then the single source of truth (ADR-035 D2) — or carries an explicit satisfied/unsatisfied mark with provenance. Persisted as one nullable JSON column with a Zod schema at the boundary (SDD-008); no new aggregate and no new model ownership, `Dependency` already belonging to the `project-manager` charter. Nullable at rest because rows predating the column exist: the invariant is enforced at the creation surface, and an edge with no contract renders as `contract undeclared` rather than looking complete (ADR-035 D6). | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
| FR-085 | Contract-gated release on the Board: a WorkItem whose inbound edges carry declared, unsatisfied contracts is **held**, and says so on its own card — naming the predecessor it waits for as a link, before the user attempts the move rather than as a refusal after it. An edge whose contract is `undeclared` (a row predating FR-084) does not hold anything: it is surfaced for backfill, never enforced retroactively, because turning a data-migration gap into a production stoppage is the worse of the two failures. | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
| FR-086 | Projects Dashboard: the Development `/projects` surface becomes the domain's Dashboard — a KPI band above the resource list — and its sidebar entry is labelled `Dashboard`, the shape every peer domain already has, so that "Overview" keeps meaning exactly one thing in this product (`/overview`, Business Home, FR-060). The band reports Projects by status, WorkItems by status, the **headcount actually working on the in-scope Projects** (distinct `WorkItem.assigneeRef`) and the **team count** from FR-089. The last two are shown as two figures because they answer different questions — people with work assigned, versus Teams attached to Projects — and neither is derivable from the other (ADR-036 D5, ADR-037 D4). Every band figure reconciles with the list beneath it: `PROJECT_STATUSES` has five values and the highlighted three are shown with an explicit account of the remainder rather than a subset that does not sum. The list carries Code · Project Name · Size · Space · Streams · Status · Progress · Target · PIC · Priority, where **Size is defined as the count of non-deleted WorkItems under the Project** (ADR-036 D2) and Progress comes from the existing pure calculators. A `Top 5 Priority Projects` panel orders by FR-087's field and, until priorities are set, renders an empty state saying so rather than substituting a deadline ordering the reader would misread as priority. `New project` becomes this page's primary action and is removed from the Topbar, where it currently duplicates a control the page already has. | `prisma/seed.js`, `app/(pm)/projects/page.jsx`, `app/api/projects/overview/route.js`, `components/layouts/Topbar.jsx`, `config/domains.js`, `config/modules.js`, `lib/validation/enums.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/projects-dashboard-read-model.js` | `e2e/fr041-business-first.spec.js`, `e2e/smoke.spec.js`, `integration/projects-dashboard-stall.test.js`, `integration/projects-dashboard.test.js`, `integration/xlsx-intake.test.js`, `unit/asset-management-navigation.test.js`, `unit/business-capability-navigation.test.js`, `unit/conversation-analysis-contract.test.js`, `unit/crm-group-navigation.test.js`, `unit/domain-navigation.test.js`, `unit/fr045-api-ui-contract.test.js`, `unit/inventory-routes.test.js`, `unit/marketing-navigation.test.js`, `unit/plan-schema.test.js`, `unit/procurement-routes.test.js`, `unit/project-list-contract.test.js`, `unit/projects-dashboard-read-model.test.js`, `unit/projects-dashboard-ui.test.js`, `unit/scm-group-navigation.test.js`, `unit/scope-view-context.test.js`, `unit/sidebar-visible-subdomains.test.js`, `unit/sign-out.test.js`, `unit/topbar-no-dropdown.test.js` | ✅ |

| FR-089 | Team as an organisational grouping: three additive Business-scoped models — `Team`, `TeamMembership` (Person ↔ Team) and `ProjectTeam` (many-to-many, because a Project is worked by several Teams and a Team works several Projects). A Team records who works together and **grants nothing**: the identity resolver does not read it, no route guard consults it, and adding a Person to a Team changes no role, no domain grant and no visible or owned Business (BR-018, ADR-037 D1). `TeamMembership` is deliberately separate from `Membership` — the same distinct-authority-layer discipline BR-016 applies to `WorkspaceMembership` — because `Membership` is the authority record `resolveViewer` reads, and merging grouping into it is how an unauthenticated POST minted business-owner authority on 2026-08-17. Work stays assigned to a Person (`WorkItem.assigneeRef`), never to a Team (ADR-037 D4). | FR-090 | Schema declaration for the live production-auth tables: `PersonCredential`, `PasswordResetToken` and `Workstream.laneId` exist on the Supabase database — `PersonCredential` with a real credential row and `laneId` with a distinct value on every Workstream — but existed in no committed schema, having been pushed there from the unmerged branch `codex/postgres-primary-runtime`. This requirement declares their shape so the schema describes the database that exists. It is deliberately **declaration only**: no service, route or UI is in scope, and the feature code remains on that branch. The reason it cannot wait for that branch to merge is that `prisma migrate diff` proposes `DROP TABLE "PersonCredential"` against an undeclared table, so any routine `db push` destroys a live credential without the operator reading the SQL. Registering the tables here removes the destructive suggestion without deciding the feature's fate. **Resolution (2026-08-26):** the branch was never merged and no longer needs to be — `main` grew its own superior implementations of everything it held (`PersonCredential` login/logout via FR-046/FR-095; password reset via FR-104, reimplemented rather than revived because the draft returned the raw reset token to the unauthenticated caller). Its FR-082/FR-083 claims stay burnt on the pipeline canvas. The branch is deleted; `Workstream.laneId` remains declared-only. | `modules/project-manager/application/backup-service.js` | `integration/backup.test.js`, `integration/crm-conversation-analysis.test.js`, `integration/fr075-restore-authorization.test.js`, `integration/line-server-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-content-backup.test.js`, `unit/fr045-backup-contract.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-091 | CRM Conversation Inbox — the reader surface for the LINE ingress. `Customer`, `Conversation` and `Message` have been written by the FR-023 ingest seam since the first LINE turn, and FR-081 declared in as many words that **no reader surface was in scope** — so every message this product has ever received is invisible to the operator whose business received it. This requirement adds that surface and nothing else. `GET /api/crm/conversations` returns one authorized, read-only list (customer, channel, message count, last-message preview and time, owning-Business label); `GET /api/crm/conversations/{id}` returns one thread with its messages oldest-first and the customer behind it. Scope follows **BR-001, not the shell**: the CRM is tenant-shared, so the list is the Tenant of the selected Business, restricted to conversations whose `businessId` is null (tenant-shared — which is what an unbound LINE binding writes, and therefore the common case) or a Business this viewer can already see. A viewer is never handed a conversation belonging to a Business they could not open. The `customer` domain slot leaves `soon` and gains `/customer` (Dashboard) and `/customer/conversations` (Inbox). It **writes nothing**: no reply box, no status transition, no read-receipt. Replying is BR-011 territory and belongs to the edge runtime that owns the reply — a console that could also reply would make two reply owners, which is the one thing BR-011 exists to prevent. `Conversation.status` is displayed as stored and no enum is declared for it, because nothing in this slice writes it. | `app/(pm)/customer/conversations/page.jsx`, `app/(pm)/customer/line-crm/page.jsx`, `app/(pm)/customer/page.jsx`, `app/api/crm/conversations/[id]/route.js`, `app/api/crm/conversations/route.js`, `config/domains.js`, `modules/crm/conversation-read-model.js`, `modules/line-crm/LineCrmAiMcp.jsx`, `modules/line-crm/LineCrmAutomation.jsx`, `modules/line-crm/LineCrmCampaigns.jsx`, `modules/line-crm/LineCrmDashboard.jsx`, `modules/line-crm/LineCrmHub.jsx`, `modules/line-crm/LineCrmLiveChat.jsx`, `modules/line-crm/LineCrmLoyalty.jsx`, `modules/line-crm/LineCrmMembers.jsx`, `modules/line-crm/LineCrmShell.jsx`, `modules/line-crm/mockData.js`, `modules/line-oa-studio/ui/LineStudioLiveCrm.jsx` | `e2e/fr041-business-first.spec.js`, `e2e/fr060-business-home.spec.js`, `e2e/fr091-conversation-inbox.spec.js`, `integration/crm-conversation-inbox.test.js`, `integration/crm-customer-consent.test.js`, `integration/line-reply-record.test.js`, `unit/asset-management-navigation.test.js`, `unit/business-capability-navigation.test.js`, `unit/command-palette-index.test.js`, `unit/conversation-read-model.test.js`, `unit/crm-group-navigation.test.js`, `unit/domain-navigation.test.js`, `unit/fr045-api-ui-contract.test.js`, `unit/fr091-inbox-ui-contract.test.js`, `unit/inventory-routes.test.js`, `unit/line-crm-live-chat-render.test.js`, `unit/marketing-navigation.test.js`, `unit/procurement-routes.test.js`, `unit/scm-group-navigation.test.js` | ✅ |

| FR-093 | LINE reply delivery receipt: the outbound half of a conversation becomes a row. Nothing has ever written a `Message` with `direction: 'OUTBOUND'` — the reply is assembled here, handed to the edge runtime, sent to the customer and then forgotten — so FR-091's inbox shows one side of every conversation and its `OUTBOUND` count is structurally zero. `POST /api/agent/line-delivery` accepts a batch of receipts from the transport owner, each naming the **inbound `Message.id` it answers** and the text that was **actually sent**. (a) The receipt comes from the edge runtime *after* a successful send and not from this side at hand-over, because the two differ: when the stack cannot answer, the transport sends its own fallback text, and recording what this side produced would record a message the customer never received (BR-011 — the reply owner is the one who knows). (b) Scope is resolved by the same seam as the webhook (FR-052), and the named inbound message must resolve inside that scope — a binding for one tenant can never attach a reply to another tenant's conversation (SEC-001). (c) Idempotent on `reply:<inboundMessageId>` as the message's external id, so a redelivered receipt resolves to the existing row: one inbound message has one reply, which is exactly what the LINE Reply API allows per token. (d) The row records the text the customer received; whether it came from the stack or from the transport's fallback is recorded on the audit event, not in the body, because the body is what was said and the provenance is a different fact. The webhook response gains `conversationId` and `inboundMessageId` so the transport has something to name — on the **failure** result as well as the success one, because a failed turn is precisely when the transport substitutes its own text and a customer certainly received something. That failure result also gains the `eventId` it never carried: the transport matches results to events by that field, so a failed result was previously unfindable and its fallback was sent only because an unmatched result and a failed one both read as "not ok". All additive; the four fields the transport already reads are untouched. | `app/api/agent/line-delivery/route.js`, `app/api/agent/line-webhook/route.js`, `modules/agent/turn.js`, `modules/crm/reply-record-service.js`, `modules/line-crm/LineCrmLiveChat.jsx`, `modules/line-oa-studio/ui/LineStudioLiveCrm.jsx` | `integration/agent-msp-thread-memory.test.js`, `integration/agent-turn.test.js`, `integration/agent-webhook-route.test.js`, `integration/line-account-isolation.test.js`, `integration/line-oa-evidence-convergence.test.js`, `integration/line-reply-record.test.js`, `integration/line-webhook-transport-contract.test.js`, `unit/line-crm-live-chat-render.test.js`, `unit/reply-record-service.test.js` | ✅ |

| FR-104 | Owner-assisted password reset: a Business owner over a Business the target Person belongs to — the same authority boundary as FR-038 Membership administration — or the installation operator mints a single-use, one-hour, hash-bound reset token (`POST /api/platform/users/password-resets`), and the public `POST /api/auth/reset-password` consumes it: new `PersonCredential`, token burnt, **every active Session revoked**. The raw token appears exactly once, in the authenticated mint response, for out-of-band handover (LINE, in person); it is stored only as a SHA-256 digest and never logged or audited in any form. There is deliberately no public forgot-password route: with no mail transport, such a route either returns the token to the unauthenticated caller — an account-takeover primitive, which is what the abandoned FR-082 draft on `codex/postgres-primary-runtime` shipped and the reason it was not revived as-is — or returns nothing and resets nothing. | FR-106 | Enterprise API tenant token authentication: the FR-019 Enterprise API refuses every request that does not present a valid per-Tenant service-account key, generalizing the FR-102 pattern (ADR-047 D3 deliberately scoped its `SotDataPlaneKey` to the two SoT routes and named this generalization as the follow-up) rather than inventing a second mechanism: an `ApiAccessKey` bound to exactly one Tenant, presented as `Authorization: Bearer`, stored only as a SHA-256 digest, minted by an installation operator or an owner in that Tenant with the raw secret shown exactly once, revocable with effect on the next request, and never readable back. | FR-108 | ExecutionPlanBundle import orchestration: the self-contained multi-Project planning package above PlanEnvelope (ADR-049; normative schema `contracts/execution-plan-bundle.schema.json`) becomes importable end to end. A Bundle Import Orchestrator in the project-manager intake lane coordinates EXISTING application services only — it writes no table directly and creates no second PlanEnvelope writer (BR-009). Scope is resolved from the trusted viewer before any sensitive parsing or preview (ADR-049 D5 — previewing another tenant's plan is refused exactly as a write would be); bundle schema and bundle-level semantics validate; strategy (Roadmap/Horizons/Goals) dry-runs; bundle-local symbolic refs resolve to canonical UUIDs inside the authorized scope and fail closed on unknown, ambiguous, cross-Business, cross-Workspace or type-mismatched targets, granting no authority (D4); every Project entry runs the EXISTING PlanEnvelope dry-run; cross-Project dependencies validate against the combined preview; and one combined preview precedes one explicit confirmation — any unresolved conflict keeps the whole bundle non-committable (D7). Commit is one database transaction in the current single-database scope (D8 atomic mode, documented), producing a bundle receipt whose `trace.idempotencyKey` binds to the normalized payload hash: a replayed key returns the prior receipt, the same key with a different hash is refused, and audit lineage connects the bundle occurrence to the per-Project receipts it caused (D9). Surfaces `POST /api/import/bundle/dry-run` and `POST /api/import/bundle/commit`; every transport converges on the orchestrator (D3). Bundles are data only — nothing arriving in one is ever executed (BR-007, SEC-002, D6). | `app/api/import/bundle/commit/route.js`, `app/api/import/bundle/dry-run/route.js`, `modules/project-manager/import/bundle/bundle-commit-service.js`, `modules/project-manager/import/bundle/bundle-dry-run.js`, `modules/project-manager/import/bundle/bundle-receipt.js`, `modules/project-manager/import/bundle/bundle-resolver.js`, `modules/project-manager/import/bundle/bundle-schema.js`, `modules/project-manager/import/plan-import-service.js` | `integration/execution-plan-bundle.test.js`, `integration/external-ref-import.test.js`, `integration/import-target-authorization.test.js`, `integration/openapi-docs.test.js`, `integration/plan-import.test.js`, `integration/project-business-binding.test.js`, `unit/bundle-schema.test.js` | ✅ |

Every ingestion occurrence is traceable end to end from a single `pipeline_job_id` — Source → RawArtifact → ParsedArtifact → Chunks → Entities → Facts → Graph → Indexes → Published Snapshot (spec §33) — with per-record disposition on `PipelineRecordEvent`, whose already-present `docId` / `picId` / `factId` columns are what that trace binds to. Sequence is documentation; the stage id is the key, exactly as FR-071's catalog already treats it, so inserting a stage never renumbers the evidence of a run that predates it. | FR-110 | Published knowledge snapshot contract: knowledge becomes readable only as a whole, identified publication — `knowledge_snapshot_id` with `tenant_id`, `business_id`, `ontology_version`, `pipeline_version`, `published_at` and object statistics (spec §25) — so a consumer can name the corpus an answer came from and two answers can be compared for whether they read the same one. Publication is atomic: a partially built index is never exposed to retrieval (spec §24), and only a Stage 17 gate result of `PASS` or `PASS_WITH_WARNINGS` may publish, while `QUARANTINE` and `FAIL` may not (spec §23). Zuri-AI consumes snapshots and records the publish-or-quarantine decision; it does not build the indexes a snapshot names (ADR-050; ADR-042 D2). | | FR-111 | Knowledge sensitivity lattice and processing policy fields: business knowledge carries a real classification instead of the single `sensitivity: z.literal('PUBLIC')` the FR-047 contract admits today (`src/modules/knowledge/business-contract.js`) — widening to the PUBLIC / INTERNAL / CONFIDENTIAL / RESTRICTED lattice and adding the per-object processing policy the pipeline must obey before it indexes anything: retention, export, `cloud_processing_allowed` and `embedding_allowed` (spec §10). Classification happens before indexing, never as a filter applied after retrieval (spec §3.3): in the index-everything-then-filter pattern a scope mistake is a disclosure rather than a ranking error, and the filter is the only thing standing between the two. RESTRICTED with `cloud_processing_allowed = false` forces all seventeen stages onto local execution (SDD-058). Extends — never replaces — the SEC-001 tenant guard and the SEC-009 deny-by-default public read boundary. | `lib/validation/enums.js`, `modules/knowledge/classification.js` | `integration/agent-request-envelope.test.js`, `integration/xlsx-intake.test.js`, `unit/agent-requestable-sensitivity.test.js`, `unit/conversation-analysis-contract.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-classification.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-stage-runner.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-zero-pii-policy.test.js`, `unit/plan-schema.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-112 | Structural knowledge chunking with parent-child lineage: a parsed document is split along its own structure — document → section → chunk — rather than at a fixed token stride, and every chunk carries the eight fields FR-109's `DPS-KI-CHUNK` catalog row already fixes (`chunk_id`, `parent_id`, `document_id`, `sequence`, `heading_path`, `token_count`, `scope`, `provenance`). `heading_path` carries the whole ancestor chain rather than the nearest heading, so a chunk retrieved on its own still says where in the document it came from. A section too large to retrieve as one unit additionally gets overlapping fixed windows beneath it, parented to the section chunk and reported as a warning — the fallback the specification names (§12), never the default; the windows overlap because without it a sentence lying across a boundary is left whole in neither. Chunking derives nothing it is given: `scope` comes from FR-111 classification and `provenance` from Stage 3, both upstream. The function is pure — no I/O, no clock, no randomness — so the same document chunks to the same ids every time, which is what lets BR-021 treat a reprocessed document as the same knowledge rather than a duplicate. Stage 7 is Tier 1 under ADR-050, so this executes here. | `modules/knowledge/chunking.js` | `unit/knowledge-chunking.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-113 | Entity candidate extraction from chunks and structured records: FR-112's chunks and caller-named record fields both yield `EntityCandidate` objects carrying the six fields FR-109's `DPS-KI-ENTITY-EXTRACT` catalog row fixes — `candidate_id`, type, mention, `normalized_name`, `source_chunk_id`, `confidence` — plus the originating chunk or record, the scope it was found in, and the provenance carried from that source so the chain back to the raw artifact survives, so a candidate can never be read outside its tenant (SEC-001, BR-001). A candidate is a mention with a guess attached and never an identity: the specification states it outright (§13, "EntityCandidate ยังไม่ใช่ canonical entity"), so two mentions of the same name stay two candidates and nothing here merges, links or resolves them — that judgement is Stage 9, which ADR-050 D2 and the specification both assign to GKS. Recognition is a seam with a deliberately narrow deterministic default (SDD-060); a record mention is read from a declared field rather than guessed, and carries confidence 1 for that reason. Pure — no I/O, no clock, no randomness, no model — so the same input yields the same candidate ids, which is what lets BR-021 treat a reprocessed document as the same knowledge. Stage 8 is Tier 1 under ADR-050, so this executes here. | `modules/knowledge/entity-extraction.js` | `unit/knowledge-entity-extraction.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-114 | Canonical normalization that never destroys the raw value: a value is returned as the pair `{ raw, canonical }`, where `raw` is the input byte for byte and `canonical` is the comparable form — the specification's §3.1 invariant that raw data stays recoverable, made structural rather than promised, and the evidence FR-109's `DPS-KI-NORMALIZE` catalog row already asks for. Six categories are normalized: Unicode and whitespace (NFC, plus the invisible spaces `s` does not match, plus the one Thai fold NFC misses — `สำ` written as NIKHAHIT + SARA AA renders identically to SARA AM and NFC leaves the two unequal), dates (Thai and Gregorian digits, four-digit Buddhist years without a hint, two-digit years only with a declared era), Thai telephone numbers to E.164, email with the domain lowercased and the local part left alone because RFC 5321 makes it case-sensitive, and organisation names by the lexical rule this requirement now owns. Stage 8 imports that rule rather than keeping a second copy, and the order is fixed and stated: normalization runs before extraction, so one mention is stripped once by one rule. Where the value does not decide, nothing is decided for it (SDD-061). Stage 4 is Tier 1 under ADR-050, so this executes here. | `modules/knowledge/normalization.js` | `unit/knowledge-normalization.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-115 | Document parsing into a structured artifact that keeps its link to the raw source: a document becomes a `ParsedArtifact` carrying `document_id`, `parsed_from`, `structure`, `text_blocks`, `tables`, `metadata` and `warnings` (spec §7 plus the warnings channel the refusals below report through), where `parsed_from` is the link back to the raw artifact FR-081 stored and `metadata` records the extractor version, so a reparse after a parser change is distinguishable from the original. This closes a gap that made the stages above it unreachable: `chunkDocument` (FR-112) consumes `blocks` and, before this, nothing in `src/` produced that shape — every caller was a test, so Stages 7 and 8 were two modules with no upstream. Parsing is deterministic and refuses structure that is not there: a `#` inside a fenced or indented code block is not a heading, `#hashtag` without a space is prose, a run of dashes with no text above it is a thematic break rather than a setext heading, a sentence containing a pipe is not a table, and a table whose separator disagrees with its header is reported and left as prose rather than read with a column dropped or invented. Markdown and plain text only; the formats needing a model or a binary keep arriving through FR-071's intake contract. Stage 2 is Tier 1 under ADR-050, so this executes here. | `modules/knowledge/parsing.js` | `unit/knowledge-parsing.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-116 | Derived-object provenance and the lineage chain back to a source: a knowledge object carries the ten fields §8 requires of anything that came from a source — `source_id`, `source_type`, `source_uri`, `source_version`, `artifact_id`, `ingested_at`, `parsed_at`, `pipeline_version`, `extractor_version`, `checksum` — and a derived object additionally names its `derivation_method` and the `source_objects` it came from, so the chain Fact → Chunk → ParsedArtifact → RawArtifact → Source can be walked and either reaches a source or says plainly that it did not. **This carries and validates a chain of identifiers it does not mint**: identity is established by FR-071's execution ledger and by Stage 2, and FR-116 consumes it — the moment it generated a `doc_id` it would have crossed into FR-071's subject rather than sitting beside it. The contract arrives after the field did: FR-112 and FR-113 have been passing a `provenance` object between them that nothing defined and nothing checked, and Stage 2 (FR-115) hands them one, and this is what gives that blob a meaning. Nothing is defaulted, an empty provenance is refused as loudly as an absent one, and an artifact parsed before it was ingested is refused because that order cannot have happened. Stage 3 is Tier 1 under ADR-050, so this executes here. | `modules/knowledge/provenance.js` | `unit/knowledge-dedup.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-provenance.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-stage-runner-trace.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-stage-runner.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-117 | Deduplication and version relationships within one tenant: an artifact is placed against the ones already held as a `DUPLICATE_OF`, a `REVISION_OF`, or independent of all of them, using BR-021's four-part ingestion identity — source identity, source version, content hash and pipeline version — computed inside a tenant and never across one (SEC-021). Both rules have carried no code anchor since they were declared; this is what gives them one. A revision emits its supersession as a PAIR, `SUPERSEDES` from the incoming artifact and `SUPERSEDED_BY` back to the one it replaced, because a graph carrying only the forward edge cannot answer "what replaced this?" from the side that was replaced, which is the side that gets asked. A duplicate emits no edge, having replaced nothing. A reparse under a changed pipeline version is a revision rather than a duplicate even when every byte matches, which is what makes reprocessing a whole corpus safe after a parser change. Nothing is defaulted: a missing component of the key is refused rather than hashed as an empty string, because a hole in a key is not an absence but a value every other artifact missing that part will collide with. `DERIVED_FROM` is never assigned here — that edge is provenance and FR-116 owns it. Stage 6 is Tier 1 under ADR-050, so this executes here. | `modules/knowledge/dedup.js` | `unit/knowledge-dedup.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-stage-runner.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-118 | Tier 1 stage composition: `runKnowledgeIngestionStages` calls the seven Tier 1 knowledge-ingestion stages — parsing (FR-115), provenance capture (FR-116), normalization (FR-114), classification (FR-111), deduplication (FR-117), chunking (FR-112), entity extraction (FR-113) — in ADR-050 D2's own order, over one artifact, in one call. Every prior slice in this lane shipped one calculator and a seam test to its one neighbour — parsing into chunking, chunking into entity extraction, classification into chunking, dedup into the FR-109 run input — four pairs, and a chain of pairs is not a chain, which the knowledge charter now states outright. This is the composition: proven by a test that runs all seven on one real artifact carrying a Thai organisation mention, a structured field and a structured record, and by three more asserting determinism (BR-021 requires the same input reprocess to the same chunk and candidate ids) and correct dedup classification (DUPLICATE_OF and REVISION_OF, not silently treated as fresh). Pure — no I/O, no persistence, no ledger write — so it closes no FR-109 acceptance criterion: AC-109.2, .4, .7 and .13 each require evidence on the FR-071 ledger, which a caller wanting it composes separately by wrapping each stage result with `recordPipelineEvent`, named here and not built. A stage's own error propagates unchanged; BR-022's quarantine vocabulary is declared and unbuilt, and catching an error with nowhere to classify it would read as handling that nothing downstream acts on. | `modules/knowledge/stage-runner.js`, `platform/integrations/core/knowledge-ingestion-executor.js` | `integration/fr109-knowledge-ingestion-executor.test.js`, `integration/fr110-knowledge-reporter-receiver.test.js`, `integration/fr119-knowledge-ingestion-quarantine.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-stage-runner-trace.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-stage-runner.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-119 | Per-stage failure attribution and BR-022 quarantine: a document that fails partway through the seven Tier 1 stages is quarantined with the complete failure envelope — `job_id`, `artifact_id`, `stage`, `error_code`, `error_message`, `retry_count`, `first_failed_at`, `last_failed_at`, `pipeline_version` — and classified retryable / non-retryable / review-required, rather than reported as nothing having happened. `runKnowledgeIngestionStagesWithTrace` extends FR-118's composition to catch each stage individually and report which succeeded before the one that did not, sharing every field mapping with the original throwing function (SDD-072) so neither duplicates the other. Every Tier 1 stage failure classifies `NON_RETRYABLE`: the seven stage functions are pure and deterministic, so a thrown validation error repeats identically on retry, and `REVIEW_REQUIRED` has no trigger either — an ambiguous value already declines via `canonical: null` (FR-114, SDD-061) rather than throwing, so it never reaches quarantine. `RETRYABLE`/`REVIEW_REQUIRED` stay in the vocabulary for failure modes this repository does not yet have (a network call, Tier 3/4 reporting) rather than being deleted for lack of a current trigger. | `modules/knowledge/quarantine.js`, `modules/knowledge/stage-runner.js`, `platform/integrations/core/knowledge-ingestion-executor.js` | `integration/fr109-knowledge-ingestion-executor.test.js`, `integration/fr110-knowledge-reporter-receiver.test.js`, `integration/fr119-knowledge-ingestion-quarantine.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-quarantine.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-stage-runner-trace.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-stage-runner.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-120 | Self-serve account creation: an unauthenticated visitor creates their own `Person` and `PersonCredential` at a public `/signup` (`POST /api/auth/signup`) and continues into FR-066 at its `PROFILE` step. This closes the product's only door. FR-066 begins *"after a provider-neutral local identity/session exists"* and never says how one comes to exist; FR-067's invite is accepted as `acceptWorkspaceInvite({ token, personId: viewer.principal.id })`, so it attaches membership to somebody already authenticated and cannot admit a stranger; and the only writers of a credential are `prisma/seed.js` and FR-107's operator bootstrap, which is operator-run and refuses an existing credential (409). Nobody new can enter — which also means nobody new can be invited, because an invite needs a Person to attach to. **Signup itself confers no authority**: it creates no `PlatformGrant`, no Tenant, Business, Space or Project, and no `WorkspaceMembership`. What the new Person may do next is exactly what FR-066 already grants any profiled Person and no more — the owner path of `createOnboardingWorkspace`, which creates one `Portfolio` plus one OWNER `WorkspaceMembership` over it and, by AC-066.2, zero Organization/Tenant/Business/Space/Project rows. Signup therefore widens who can reach that path; it does not widen the path. Entry to an existing team remains FR-067's invite, and the resting state for a Person holding nothing is FR-066's Waiting Room. Signup never overwrites an existing `PersonCredential` — the same refusal FR-107 makes, for the same reason. Password validation and hashing are the FR-046/FR-104 path's `hashPassword`, called rather than copied, so a policy change cannot apply to one door and not the other; the human `PSN-` code comes from the same `uniqueHumanCode` generator (BR-002); and the session is FR-046's minting path, not a second implementation. **The caller is signed in on success** rather than returned to `/login` to retype the password they just chose, and the cookie is a browser-session cookie — signup carries no "remember me" tick, so it takes AC-046-15's default rather than opting itself into seven days. Creation writes an audit event. Identity stays provider-neutral and local: no SSO or third-party provider is in scope. **Email is an identifier here, not a channel.** This installation has no mail transport — the stated reason FR-104 has no public forgot-password — so there is no verification step and no "check your inbox" response to hide a taken address behind. A taken email therefore returns a distinguishable error instead of stranding the caller in silence. That enumeration is accepted deliberately: with no mail the alternative is a signup that fails invisibly, and learning that an address holds an account grants the learner nothing, because that account carries no scope, no capability and no membership until its holder creates a Workspace or an owner invites them. The compensating controls are the audit trail and a per-source rate limit — and that limit is **in-process and per-instance**: a fixed window held in the Node process's own memory, which resets on restart and does not span replicas. Stated rather than glossed, because a control described more strongly than it is built is worse than one described honestly. An unverified address must never be treated by any later requirement as proof of contact. | `app/api/auth/signup/route.js`, `app/login/page.jsx`, `app/signup/page.jsx`, `modules/identity/auth-service.js`, `modules/identity/signup-copy.js`, `modules/identity/signup-rate-limit.js`, `modules/identity/signup-service.js` | `e2e/fr046-entry-contract.spec.js`, `e2e/fr104-password-reset-redemption.spec.js`, `e2e/fr120-signup.spec.js`, `integration/openapi-docs.test.js`, `integration/password-reset-flow.test.js`, `unit/auth-service.test.js`, `unit/entry-surfaces.test.js`, `unit/fr046-api-ui-contract.test.js`, `unit/fr046-auth-route.test.js`, `unit/fr120-signup-page.test.js`, `unit/fr120-signup-rate-limit.test.js`, `unit/fr120-signup-route.test.js`, `unit/fr120-signup-service.test.js`, `unit/iam-session.test.js`, `unit/password-reset-service.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-121 | Google as a second way in, over the same `Person`: a visitor may present a Google account at `/signup` and `/login`, and the OAuth callback resolves the provider `sub` to exactly one internal `Person` through a namespaced external binding (FR-094) — a second way to prove who you are, never a second account path. Sign-in follows sign-up necessarily: a person who signed up with Google set no password, so refusing Google at `/login` would strand the account behind a credential that was never created. The account it produces grants nothing, exactly as FR-120: no `PlatformGrant`, Tenant, Business, Space, Project or `WorkspaceMembership`. **It does not satisfy FR-122** — Google supplies an address and a display name of its own choosing, and neither is the given name, family name or telephone number a Profile requires; the telephone number Google never supplies at all. An address matching an existing local account binds to that `Person` **only when Google asserts `email_verified`**; an unverified assertion is refused rather than linked, because linking on an unverified address is account takeover performed by signing up. **Declared blocked, not merely unbuilt**, and on two named things rather than on effort: this installation holds no OAuth client credential, and `ExternalIdentity` is keyed `(tenantId, provider, providerSubject)` with `tenantId` a required foreign key — which a self-serve signup deliberately does not have (FR-120 creates no Tenant), so the binding needs a tenant-less form before any of this can be written. | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
| FR-122 | A Profile states who the person is, not just what to call them: FR-066's Profile step requires **given name, family name and telephone number**, over the display name it already collected and the optional email it already carried. Display name stops being something the person types twice — it is supplied by them or defaulted server-side from given + family name, never left empty, because `Person.displayName` is what every existing surface renders. The three new fields are stored **nullable on `Person`** and required **at the profile boundary instead**, and the distinction is load-bearing rather than a convenience: `Person` rows already exist that can never satisfy them — `prisma/seed.js`, FR-107's operator bootstrap, and above all every Person that FR-023's LINE ingest creates on first contact, which is created from a `lineUserId` and has no name or telephone number to give. A database-level requirement would make those rows unwritable and take the LINE intake surface down with it; the profile step is the only place a person states these things themselves, so it is the only place the requirement can be honestly enforced. Profile remains an identity step over `Person`, never an authorization grant (FR-066, BR-016). | `app/(entry)/onboarding/profile/page.jsx`, `app/api/onboarding/profile/route.js`, `modules/identity/onboarding-service.js` | `integration/workspace-onboarding-flow.test.js`, `unit/fr123-plugin-auth-migration.test.js`, `unit/onboarding-service.test.js`, `unit/profile-identity-fields-migration.test.js`, `unit/workspace-onboarding-routes.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-123 | Plugin authentication and capability discovery: first-party Codex/Claude Code harnesses obtain authority through a server-owned authorization-code flow under `/api/plugin/auth` — a signed-in browser session authorizes a 60-second single-use code bound to an exact `client_id`, an exact registered `redirect_uri`, an `installation_id` and a PKCE S256 challenge; exchanging it with the verifier yields a 15-minute opaque bearer session. Codes and tokens are persisted as SHA-256 hashes only, so a database read recovers nothing usable. Capabilities are **derived** from `resolveViewer` and are advisory UX: reads for any visible Business, `requiresApproval` writes only where the person owns one, and every command re-authorizes on its own path before mutating anything. The plugin never submits a human password, copies the `zuri_session` cookie, uses `Mcp-Session-Id` as authorization, connects to the database, or self-asserts Tenant/Business/Membership/role scope; it also never inherits a platform DEV grant, so installing a plugin cannot widen what the person could already reach. Redirect matching is exact and stays exact — `localhost` and `127.0.0.1` are two registrations, a decision forced by a Preview transport that rewrites one into the other (ADR-052 D1). Replaying a consumed code is treated as evidence the code leaked after use: it is refused **and** revokes the session that code already minted. Revoke is idempotent and answers identically for a token that never existed. Distinct from FR-106's `ApiAccessKey`, which is a long-lived Tenant-bound service credential; this is a minutes-long delegation from one person to one installation (ADR-052). Authorization is a two-step gate: `GET /authorize` renders a consent screen and mints nothing, and only a POST from that screen's form — carrying the session cookie, a session-bound anti-CSRF token and an HMAC-signed request token whose parameters are the ones displayed — mints a code. Refusal returns `error=access_denied` with the original `state` (ADR-052 D4). | `app/(entry)/plugin/authorize/page.jsx`, `app/api/plugin/auth/authorize/route.js`, `app/api/plugin/auth/capabilities/route.js`, `app/api/plugin/auth/revoke/route.js`, `app/api/plugin/auth/token/route.js`, `lib/viewer-failure.js`, `modules/identity/plugin-auth-service.js`, `modules/identity/plugin-consent-access.js`, `modules/identity/plugin-consent-view.js`, `modules/identity/plugin-consent.js`, `modules/project-manager/api-docs/openapi.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/backup-service.js` | `e2e/fr123-plugin-consent.spec.js`, `integration/backup.test.js`, `integration/crm-conversation-analysis.test.js`, `integration/fr075-restore-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr123-plugin-auth-reaper.test.js`, `integration/fr123-plugin-consent-gate.test.js`, `integration/line-server-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-content-backup.test.js`, `integration/openapi-docs.test.js`, `unit/fr045-backup-contract.test.js`, `unit/fr123-plugin-auth-migration.test.js`, `unit/fr123-plugin-auth-route.test.js`, `unit/fr123-plugin-auth-service.test.js`, `unit/fr123-plugin-consent-view.test.js`, `unit/fr123-plugin-consent.test.js`, `unit/viewer-failure.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-124 | Product Readiness: one read-only Platform surface at `/platform/product-readiness`, with a stable drilldown at `/platform/product-readiness/[domain]`, that answers four questions separately rather than collapsing them into one green number — how much evidence-backed implementation exists, which requirements are actually verified, whether a complete feature is ready to use, and what a Human can use it for. It renders the committed `docs/.domain-state.json` projection and nothing else: no database model, no runtime write path, no scheduled poll, no external API. **Readiness is not progress.** A feature is ready only when every underlying FR is verified and, for an explicit FEAT bundle, the registry says `live`; a 100% implementation score therefore still reads not-ready when the registry says `building`, and the surface always prints both numbers with the blocking reason. Progress itself is weighted 20% declaration + 40% code + 40% test — **a policy choice, not a derived fact**, held in one named constant (`PROGRESS_METHODOLOGY` in `scripts/domain-state.mjs`), printed beside the numbers in the UI and carried in the snapshot, and awaiting owner sign-off rather than inheritance. Projection is complete by construction: every FEAT bundle and every unbundled FR appears exactly once with one hand-written use case, and missing, duplicate, unknown or use-case-less metadata aborts graph generation, because a partial list is a wrong answer. The surface resolves its viewer on the server before rendering, so the snapshot never reaches an unauthorized browser through the RSC payload. | `app/(pm)/platform/product-readiness/[domain]/page.jsx`, `app/(pm)/platform/product-readiness/page.jsx`, `config/domains.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/product-readiness-access.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/product-readiness-read-model.js`, `modules/project-manager/components/ProductReadinessDashboard.jsx` | `e2e/fr041-business-first.spec.js`, `e2e/fr124-product-readiness.spec.js`, `unit/asset-management-navigation.test.js`, `unit/business-capability-navigation.test.js`, `unit/crm-group-navigation.test.js`, `unit/domain-navigation.test.js`, `unit/domain-state.test.js`, `unit/fr045-api-ui-contract.test.js`, `unit/fr124-product-readiness-read-model.test.js`, `unit/fr124-product-readiness-ui.test.js`, `unit/inventory-routes.test.js`, `unit/marketing-navigation.test.js`, `unit/procurement-routes.test.js`, `unit/scm-group-navigation.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-125 | FlowAccount read-only pull pipeline: an OWNER-authorized Business can onboard one FlowAccount Client Credentials connection through a provider-specific Platform wizard that accepts Client ID/Client Secret write-only, provisions them into the approved secret manager, verifies `GET /company/info`, and returns redacted metadata only. A server-owned `FLOWACCOUNT` adapter exchanges/reuses Bearer tokens, calls only the approved GET resource catalog, paginates within the provider page-size ceiling, respects provider account rate limits, and sends every record through the existing FR-081 `zIngestionEnvelope`/`RawExternalRecord` path with trusted Tenant/Business/connection scope, external identity, payload hash, IngestionRun, SyncCursor and DeadLetterRecord evidence. Cursor advancement requires a complete resource run; document incrementals use a lookback window and master resources use full hash reconciliation because FlowAccount exposes no generic updated-since cursor. The UI exposes computed data-source health and run summaries, never raw secret/token/payload. Public OpenAPI evidence is not GL/TB/P&L or Accounting truth; provider writes, OpenID, webhook, scheduler and downstream domain publication are separate future requirements. Design is ADR-053 | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
| FR-126 | AI-inferred Customer Profile: a 1:0..1 advisory `CustomerProfile` row on Customer holding attributes the agent runtime infers from conversation content — demographic band, occupation, motivations, budget signal — with `inferenceCount` and `lastInferredAt` as provenance. The shape is borrowed from the legacy ERD as prior art and rebound (ADR-054 D3): it hangs off the tenant-scoped Customer and adds no scope column of its own. It is derived data, never identity — `Person`/`ChannelIdentity` remain the only identity truth (FR-094), the profile performs no channel merging (ADR-054 D4), and it must be regenerable from retained conversations at any time (ADR-054 D6). It is personal data under SEC-005: reads sit behind the same BR-001 visibility as the FR-091 inbox, and PDPA erasure of the Customer removes the profile with it. One writer in crm `application/`, beside the ingest seam it never widens | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
| FR-127 | Conversation intelligence analysis: a per-Conversation, per-analysis-run `ConversationAnalysis` record classifying the exchange — contact type, engagement state, observed call-to-action, tags and a short summary, with the raw model output retained for audit. Keyed to `Conversation.id` and never to the external thread id (BR-002; ADR-054 D4 makes the legacy G-DB-02 gotcha structural rather than disciplinary); state and type vocabularies are string enums owned by `src/lib/validation/enums.js` when implementation lands. Derived and recomputable from `Message` rows, so deleting and re-running analysis is always safe (ADR-054 D6). The producer is the agent runtime, which owns no models by design; the rows and their single writer live under the crm charter (ADR-054 D2). | FR-128 | Daily Sales Brief: one aggregate row per (Business, briefDate) summarizing that day's FR-127 analyses — conversation and contact counts, engagement-state breakdown, top calls-to-action and top tags — with a `PENDING → PROCESSED → SENT / FAILED` delivery lifecycle. Aggregates are recomputed from FR-127 rows, never incremented in place, and the row is a delivery record, not a second source of truth (ADR-054 D6). Delivery is a LINE push through the existing outbound transport ownership — BR-011 stands and this feature adds no second LINE writer — to recipients resolved from Membership authority for the Business, never a hardcoded list. Scope is carried as `(businessId, briefDate)` with `tenantId` denormalized the way `Branch` carries it (ADR-054 D3) | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
| FR-129 | Catalog publication approval gate: a `DPL-SUPABASE-BUSINESS-KNOWLEDGE-V1` run that has produced a candidate projection does not reach `DPS-PUBLISH` until an authorized human has recorded an `APPROVED` `PipelineGateDecision` against that run, and a `REJECTED` decision sends it to `DPS-ROLLBACK` instead — the two stage labels FR-071's catalog has always carried ("Publish approved projection", "Rollback failed or rejected run") with nothing yet deciding which one a run takes. (a) The decision is recorded on the existing FR-071 execution ledger and declares **no new model and no new column**: `PipelineGateDecision.decidedByPersonId` is who signed, that row's own `createdAt` is when, `required` is whether the gate was waivable, `reason` carries a rejection, and `PipelineRun.artifactRef`/`artifactSha256` already identify the artifact the run applied. A second run table as CR-003 proposes is refused: `IngestionRun` (FR-081) is acquisition evidence for one connection and `PipelineRun` (FR-071) is the execution ledger for one pipeline definition, and a third answer to "what happened in this pipeline" would make the reconciliation evidence something that has to be believed twice (SDD-057). (b) The evidence a reviewer acted on — the candidate publication's own identity, and the added/changed/unchanged counts shown — is written to `PipelineGateDecision.evidenceJson` (SDD-075); a signature whose evidence is empty records that somebody approved but not what they saw, which is not an auditable decision. (c) The destination vault is not stored: it resolves from the run's Tenant/Business scope through API-010 (FR-057, ADR-022), so a stored copy would be a second answer to which vault a scope maps to. (d) Tier 1 records and does not execute (ADR-043 D2.1, ADR-050 D3). The gate is therefore a precondition the executing worker must observe and the ledger makes checkable afterwards — a run whose publish stage succeeded with no prior `APPROVED` decision is a detectable violation — not an interlock this repository enforces on a worker it does not run. (e) **Distinct from FR-110's Stage 17 quality gate**, permanently and by design: FR-110 is an automated `PASS`/`PASS_WITH_WARNINGS` verdict on a `DPL-KNOWLEDGE-INGEST-V1` knowledge snapshot, this is a human signature on a business-knowledge catalog version, and both will answer to "approval gate on a pipeline run" in any future search while sharing the one `PipelineGateDecision` table. Reworked from CR-003; no reviewer UI is in scope (CR-003 §2.B is a feature programme, not this requirement) | `platform/integrations/core/pipeline-gate-compliance.js`, `platform/integrations/core/pipeline-tracking-contract.js`, `platform/integrations/core/pipeline-tracking-service.js` | `integration/fr129-catalog-publication-gate.test.js`, `unit/asset-management-pipeline-contract.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-published-snapshot-contract.test.js`, `unit/platform/fr129-catalog-publication-gate.test.js`, `unit/platform/knowledge-ingestion-catalog.test.js`, `unit/platform/pipeline-tracking-contract.test.js`, `unit/platform/pipeline-tracking-service.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-130 | Business-scoped GitHub repository projection: a `Repository` a Business already owns may additionally name its GitHub origin, and a person the Business admits may read that repository's tree and file contents in the console, read-only. (a) The binding declares **no new model, no new column and no migration**, because the entity already exists: FR-008 created `Repository` and FR-073 made it owned by exactly one Business, which is the scope CR-004 also chose and the only scope the viewer contract can govern (SDD-037). CR-004's `githubRepoUrl` is `Repository.url` beside `ownerName`/`repoName`/`fullName`/`provider`; its `githubBranch` is `Repository.defaultBranch`, with `ProjectRepository.branch` as the per-link override; GitHub's numeric repository id is `Repository.externalRepoId` mapped through `ExternalEntityRef` — the identifier BR-002 names by example ("GitHub id") and forbids as a key; and CR-004's three sync fields are **not stored at all**, because they are artifacts of a design this one does not adopt: `lastCommitSha`, `lastGithubSyncAt` and `githubSyncStatus` describe a mirror kept in step with an origin, and a read-through projection (d) keeps no mirror — the commit a reader is looking at comes from the same live fetch that produced the tree, so storing it would be a second answer to a question the response already carries. Should a cache ever be added, the watermark is `SyncCursor.cursorValue` with `lastSuccessAt` beside it and `IngestionRun.status` for the outcome, and that is the point at which its keying must be settled rather than assumed: `SyncCursor` is unique on `(connectionId, resourceType)`, so one connection covering several repositories has no per-repository cursor without either compounding `resourceType` or narrowing a connection to one repository — and `externalAccountId` names an *account*, which is why `Repository` is a separate model in the first place. Five columns on `Business` are refused: `Business` is a scope-chain entity, columns on it would cap a Business at one repository where `Repository` already permits many, and a foreign URL and commit SHA on a core scope row is the shape BR-002 exists to prevent. (b) A private repository's credential is an `IntegrationCredential.secretRef` on a `github` `IntegrationConnection`, resolved through the `SecretManagerPort` — a reference, never material, never in a browser response (SDD-043, SEC-015). Registering the provider row is idempotent on its code and needs no migration. (c) Push delivery is an **FR-081 adapter, not a new endpoint**: a webhook is one of the four `SOURCE_TYPES` that requirement already names, and a bespoke `/api/webhooks/github` would be the second raw-write path FR-081 forbids by name — the same finding CR-005's LINE connector drew. The tenant CR-004 omits is never read from the payload: the delivery's `installation.id` selects exactly one `IntegrationConnection` on `(providerId, externalAccountId)` and **zero or multiple candidates fail closed** (the FR-079 rule), the connection carries the trusted `tenantId`/`businessId`, and `repository.id` then resolves to a `Repository` through `ExternalEntityRef`. The authentication CR-004 omits is GitHub's `X-Hub-Signature-256` — HMAC-SHA256 over the raw body under the connection's webhook secret, constant-time compared, verified before anything is parsed for meaning or written; routing reads `installation.id` only, which is a non-secret selector the signature then proves, so trust follows verification rather than preceding it. (d) **File content is never persisted** — only push metadata converges on the raw substrate, and the tree and blob reads that render a file are read-through with no row of their own (SDD-076). Writes to `Repository` continue to go through project-manager's `repository-service`, which stays its only writer; this requirement reads that model and does not claim it. (e) The projection is bounded by a declared path scope, deny-by-default in the shape SEC-009 already uses, and `vaults/`-class live database state is excluded rather than discovered by a previewer (CR-006 §5.2). No file tree UI, purpose badge, multi-format previewer or metadata panel is in scope: CR-004 §2.B is a four-part feature programme, not this requirement. Reworked from CR-004 | `app/(pm)/platform/integrations/page.jsx`, `platform/integrations/core/connector-catalog.js` | `e2e/fr130-connector-catalog.spec.js`, `unit/fr080-ui-contract.test.js`, `unit/integrations-page-claims.test.js`, `unit/platform/connector-catalog.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-131 | Shipping rate card as governed business knowledge: a Business's logistics rate matrix — Member tier × warehouse × shipping method × category, each cell a THB/CBM rate and a THB/kg rate with the CBM and weight minima that qualify a shipment for that tier — is published as `business_knowledge` rows under a **second `knowledge_type`**, and declares **no Prisma model, no new domain and no settings write path**. (a) The rows go where the same Business's product prices already go. `zuri_core.business_knowledge` is Tenant- and Business-scoped under forced RLS behind a `SET LOCAL ROLE` login (SDD-026) and already carries every fact a rate needs: `as_of` for the sheet's effective date, `approved_at`, `source_ref` and `source_sha256` for the supplier rate sheet the numbers came from, `is_active` for supersession, plus `unit`, `sell_price` and a `specification` object for the per-kilogram rate and the tier minima. CR-005's `ShippingRateMatrix` Prisma model is refused for the reason SDD-077 gives, and additionally because no chartered domain may hold it: `knowledge` owns no Prisma models by design and has kept that boundary through FR-109…FR-119, and `market-intelligence` reserves commercial state for a "Commerce authority" that has no charter in this repository. (b) Rates enter through the intake already built for this exact input. The sheets are supplier documents, so `SMARTGIFT_DOCUMENT_INTAKE` stages them (`document-intake-contract.js` accepts image sources), `DPL-SUPABASE-BUSINESS-KNOWLEDGE-V1` publishes them, and FR-129's `APPROVED` `PipelineGateDecision` is what makes a rate change readable. The administrator-editable grid of CR-005 §3.B.1 is refused on that ground rather than on effort: a rate a person may silently retype has no effective date, no signature and no source, and a quotation priced from it cannot be explained afterwards. (c) The density switch — charge by weight at or above 400 kg/CBM, by volume below — is a property of the published card, not of a cell, and is carried once per version rather than copied onto every cell, where sixty-four copies are sixty-four chances to disagree. (d) **Sell-side only.** FR-047 excludes cost and margin from this projection by name; what a customer is charged belongs here and the cost basis behind CR-005 §4.1's margin floor does not (FR-132 (c)). (e) Scope is `(tenant_id, business_id)` and not the `workspaceId` CR-005 proposes: `Workspace.tenantId` and `Workspace.businessId` are both nullable under `scopeType` `PORTFOLIO`/`TENANT`/`BUSINESS`, and a LINE turn resolves `{tenantId, businessId}` from its server-owned binding (FR-052) and never holds a `workspaceId` at all, so a workspace-keyed rate is unreadable from the one surface that needs it. Reworked from CR-005; the quoting behaviour is FR-132 | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
| FR-132 | Ladder quotation on the LINE surface, as a Gate E read-only tool: a customer asking for a price inside an existing LINE conversation ("ขอใบเสนอราคา", "คำนวณราคา TDD03-2 จำนวน 100 ชิ้น") gets a tiered quotation in the reply, and this declares **no route, no model, no converter and no credential**. (a) It adds nothing to the intake path because that path is already complete. The FR-028 webhook receives an already-verified, already-normalized batch from `zuri.command-agent`; `normalizeLineWebhookEvent` already converts every event onto the FR-081 ingestion envelope; `handleAgentTurn` already runs the turn. CR-005's `/api/connectors/line-oa/[businessId]` is refused as the second write path BR-009 and SDD-009 forbid by name — and so is the *converter* a first reading of that refusal implies, because the converter exists and has since FR-081. What is missing is one descriptor in `defaultReadOnlyTools`, which registers cleanly at Gate E precisely because a quotation computes and writes nothing (`createToolRegistry` rejects any descriptor whose `readOnly` is not `true`). (b) The reply is **returned** to the sole transport owner, never sent: BR-011 gives `zuri.command-agent` signature verification and the Reply API, and FR-050 gives this repository at most one reply text per event and no `replyToken`. The LINE Flex Message of CR-005 §3.B.2 is therefore the transport's rendering of the answer this repository returns, not something this repository posts — the boundary FR-128 already keeps for the Daily Sales Brief. (c) CR-005 §4's two invariants are both kept, and split by who may see them: rounding up to the nearest 10 THB is part of the quoted number and belongs in the reply, while the margin floor is a cost-side test applied before the number is returned and never carried in the payload, because FR-047 excludes margin from what this surface may read. A quotation that showed its own floor would breach that exclusion in the one place a customer is reading. (d) Authorization is the registry's own: `requireToolAuthorization` resolves `{tenantId, businessId, principalId}` from the turn's AuthContext and calls `authorizeScope`, so a quote is priced from the asking Business's rate card and from no other. (e) An MCP tool as CR-005 §3.B.3 proposes is a **different surface, not this one**, and is neither refused nor part of this requirement: `/api/mcp` is a viewer-authenticated back-office transport whose registry (`TOOL_DEFINITIONS`) shares no code with the in-process Gate E registry a LINE turn binds. Reworked from CR-005 | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
