| FR-141 | Edge Device heartbeat registry — the cloud-side liveness view ADR-041 D3 promises the console, and nothing more: `GET`, `POST` and `DELETE /api/agent/heartbeat` each resolve one trusted viewer before any work and fail closed (a missing or invalid session is 401, never an anonymous device list). A heartbeat is a validated `{businessId, deviceId, status, contractVersion, registeredQueries, approvedTemplates, engine?, model?}` payload: a failed parse is 400 carrying the issues and registers nothing (there is no default device), and `businessId` must be a Business the viewer **owns** (`ownsBusiness`, 403 otherwise). The registry is keyed by `(businessId, deviceId)`; a viewer lists and removes only devices of Businesses they own, and `DELETE` without `deviceId` clears exactly that owned scope. `deviceToken` is accepted on the wire for compatibility and is never stored, returned or audited (ADR-041 D3: zero edge secrets in the cloud). A device is `online` when its last heartbeat is within 120 s and its status is `healthy`. First registration, every status transition and every removal write an `EDGE_DEVICE` audit event; a tick that changes nothing writes none. Every failure returns its real status code through the shared `handle` helper. Liveness lives in a process-local registry **by decision**: the agent charter owns no Prisma model, a heartbeat is a cache that goes stale in two minutes rather than a record, and the cost is stated rather than hidden — the registry is empty after a cold start and is per-instance on Vercel, so the console reads "not paired" until the next tick reaches the instance that serves it. Durable pairing (a device record with a token reference) is a separate declaration this FR does not make. | `app/api/agent/heartbeat/route.js`, `modules/agent/edge-device-registry.js` | `e2e/edge-desktop-ui.spec.js`, `integration/agent-heartbeat-route.test.js`, `unit/fr141-edge-device-heartbeat.test.js`, `unit/integrations-page-claims.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-142 | Deployment liveness probe and public-origin abstraction (ADR-058): `GET /api/health` answers without a session, runs exactly one trivial query (`SELECT 1`) against the configured application database, and returns `{status:'ok', db:'ok'}` with 200 or `{status:'degraded', db:'unreachable'}` with 503 — states, counts and timings only, never an error message, host or credential (SEC-009). It is the probe Docker Compose uses to mark the `web` container healthy and to start ngrok only after it. The deployment's public origin is one run-time variable, `PUBLIC_BASE_URL` (`src/lib/public-base-url.js`: `PUBLIC_BASE_URL` → build-time `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` → `http://localhost:3100`, reduced to an origin; a non-HTTP or unparsable value is ignored, not propagated); the root layout's `metadataBase` and the Platform Integrations pairing payload and LINE webhook URL derive from it or from the origin the page was actually served from — no platform hostname is assumed anywhere. | `app/api/health/route.js`, `app/layout.jsx`, `lib/public-base-url.js` | `integration/openapi-docs.test.js`, `unit/entry-routing-boundary.test.js`, `unit/fr142-health-route.test.js`, `unit/public-base-url.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-143 | Edge-executed asset evidence extraction (ADR-059 — the pull model): a cloud-queued `AssetExtractionJob` lets the customer-premise Zuri Edge Device (ADR-041) run the FR-138 candidate extraction under its own subscription-based auth, so neither a provider API key nor any edge secret has to live in the cloud (ADR-041 D3) and the cloud never has to reach a device behind NAT. `POST /api/assets/evidence/{id}/extract` chooses the provider — `ZURI_ASSET_EVIDENCE_PROVIDER` = `openai` \| `edge` wins when set; unset resolves to `edge` when no `OPENAI_API_KEY` is configured **and** the Business has at least one ACTIVE FR-144 credential, otherwise `openai` — where `openai` is FR-138 unchanged and `edge` creates exactly one QUEUED job and answers `202 { job }`. The lifecycle is QUEUED → CLAIMED → COMPLETED \| FAILED, plus CANCELLED, plus lease expiry back to QUEUED, with at most one non-terminal job per `AssetEvidence`. The device claims work with the FR-144 credential: `POST /api/edge/extraction-jobs/claim` returns the oldest QUEUED job of that credential's Business under a 10-minute lease or `204` when none is queued; `GET /api/edge/extraction-jobs/{id}/evidence` streams the evidence bytes with the `FileAsset` MIME **only** while the job is CLAIMED by that device and the lease is live, never a bucket URL or storage credential; `POST /api/edge/extraction-jobs/{id}/complete` `{candidate, model}` and `POST /api/edge/extraction-jobs/{id}/fail` `{reason}` close it. The returned candidate is validated with the **same** `zCandidate` schema the OpenAI adapter uses — one shared module, so the two adapters cannot drift — and written through the same path as `extractAssetEvidence`: `AssetEvidence.extractionJson`, status `EXTRACTED`, one `ASSET_EVIDENCE_EXTRACTED` audit event carrying provider `edge`, the model the device reported and the device id. Extraction creates a candidate and never a review or an approval (BR-025 stands). `complete`/`fail` are idempotent per job `version`, a COMPLETED job is never re-claimed, and `GET /api/assets/evidence/{id}/extraction-job` gives the review surface the latest job to poll. | `app/api/assets/evidence/[id]/extract/route.js`, `app/api/assets/evidence/[id]/extraction-job/route.js`, `app/api/edge/extraction-jobs/[id]/complete/route.js`, `app/api/edge/extraction-jobs/[id]/evidence/route.js`, `app/api/edge/extraction-jobs/[id]/fail/route.js`, `app/api/edge/extraction-jobs/claim/route.js`, `modules/asset-management/application/asset-extraction-job-service.js`, `modules/asset-management/application/asset-extraction-job-view.js`, `modules/asset-management/application/asset-extraction-provider.js`, `modules/asset-management/infrastructure/asset-evidence-candidate-schema.js`, `modules/project-manager/api-docs/openapi.js` | `integration/fr143-asset-extraction-job.test.js`, `integration/openapi-docs.test.js`, `unit/asset-evidence-extractor-contract.test.js`, `unit/asset-evidence-route-schema-contract.test.js`, `unit/asset-extraction-job-view.test.js`, `unit/asset-extraction-provider.test.js`, `unit/edge-extraction-job-contract.test.js`, `unit/edge-extraction-poller.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-144 | Edge device credential — `EdgeDeviceCredential`, the Business-scoped bearer credential a Zuri Edge Device presents to the cloud. It is a third, distinct credential family: FR-106's `ApiAccessKey` is Tenant-bound Enterprise API access and FR-123's `PluginSession` is a public-client plugin identity; neither is a device. A Business OWNER (`ownsBusiness`) or an installation operator mints one from LINE OA Studio's Edge Connection page (moved 2026-09-09 from `/platform/integrations`, which now carries no pairing UI of its own) with `POST /api/platform/edge-devices/credentials` `{businessId, deviceId, label}`: the raw `edgk_…` key appears **exactly once**, in the mint response, and is persisted only as a SHA-256 `keyHash` beside a display `keyPrefix`, with `status` ACTIVE \| REVOKED, `lastUsedAt`, `revokedAt` and `revokeReason`. `GET /api/platform/edge-devices/credentials?businessId=` returns metadata only and `DELETE /api/platform/edge-devices/credentials/{id}` revokes; mint and revoke each write an `EDGE_DEVICE_CREDENTIAL` audit event, and a Business the caller does not own is refused with the same 404 a nonexistent Business receives (FR-072 refusal discipline). `resolveEdgeDeviceContext(request)` turns `Authorization: Bearer edgk_…` into `{ deviceId, businessId, tenantId, credentialId }` and touches `lastUsedAt`, or refuses `401` — missing, malformed, unknown, revoked and non-`edgk_` credentials are indistinguishable in the response. It is the authority for the FR-143 device routes and is additionally accepted by the FR-141 heartbeat `POST`, closing that requirement's open "device-scoped credential" item: the device context supplies `businessId`, and a `businessId` in the payload must match it or be absent. Owner-approved 2026-09-08: `/edge/pair` adds browser/QR approval through `POST /api/edge/pairing/start`, `/approve` and `/poll`: five-minute, bounded process-local requests; authenticated same-origin Business-owner approval; separate initiating-device secret; authority rechecked and existing credential minted only on one-time device redemption. Browser receives no device key; restart/expiry/cancellation requires a fresh request. | FR-146 | LINE OA Studio account aggregate — `LineOaAccount`, the Business-scoped operating record for one LINE Official Account and the root of the multi-account command center (ADR-060 D2/D3/D5/D11). Identity: internal UUID plus a Tenant-unique human `code`; `tenantId` and `businessId` derived from the trusted viewer and the selected visible Business, never from the payload; a 1:1 reference to an existing `LINE_OA` `IntegrationConnection` (FR-080); an optional agent binding `code` (= identity's `channelAccountId`, FR-052 / FR-097); LINE channel id, basic id and bot user id are external references (BR-002), never keys. State: `status` DRAFT → CONNECTED → LIVE \| PAUSED \| ARCHIVED, where LIVE is derived from the agent lane's ACTIVE binding and can never be set by a client; `transportMode` EDGE \| CLOUD, fixed at connect time from whether the Business holds an ACTIVE `EdgeDeviceCredential` (FR-144, the ADR-059 D5 rule), overridable by a publisher at connect time and changed afterwards only by an audited, versioned switch that cancels work queued under the old owner; `isDefaultForBusiness` at most one per Business; a presentation profile (greeting, fallback text, persona label) that is configuration, not knowledge. Cardinality: any number of accounts per Business, exactly one Business per account; moving an account is a new account plus an archive. Authority: list and read require Business visibility plus the `line-oa` domain (FR-061); connect, pause, archive, default and mode override require Business OWNER or an active `LINE_OA_PUBLISHER` `RoleBinding` (FR-076 pattern); refusals are 404-shaped (FR-072). Connecting never creates, reads or returns credential material and never activates routing (ADR-020): the connection is created only through the integration lane's owner-only contract, and health (connection status and secret readiness, binding status, last webhook receipt, queued or failed transport jobs, quota) is computed read-model output, never stored. Every write is transactional, versioned and audited without secret or customer content. | `app/(pm)/line-oa/design-studio/page.jsx`, `app/(pm)/line-oa/edge-connection/page.jsx`, | FR-147 | LINE binding status read contract — a read-only, Tenant/Business-scoped reader over the agent lane's `zuri_core.line_channel_binding` (`createPostgresLineBindingStatusReader`, `src/modules/agent/line-binding-status.js`) that answers one question, "is there a currently valid ACTIVE binding with this code for this Tenant and Business", and nothing else. It selects `code`, `status`, `valid_from`, `expires_at`, `rotated_at` and `version` and never a hash or credential column; runs through the same `zuri_line_smartgift_ro` read role, short transaction and forced-RLS path as the Phase 1 runtime (FR-052, SEC-010); and takes Tenant, Business and code from the caller's server-owned row (FR-146's account), never from a request. Because that role's policy exposes only ACTIVE, in-window rows of the scope it is granted for, the contract has exactly four honest labels: `ACTIVE` (a row is visible and ACTIVE), `NOT_ACTIVE` (a reader is configured and no such row is visible — pending, inactive, expired, rotated, absent and out-of-policy are indistinguishable by design), `NO_BINDING` (the account carries no binding code), and `UNKNOWN` (no reader is configured: `ZURI_LINE_BUSINESS_AGENT_ENABLED` is not `true` or `ZURI_LINE_DB_URL` is unset — the dev/test default). FR-146's account health reports the label with its source, and `effectiveStatus` reaches `LIVE` only on `ACTIVE`. The contract activates, rotates or expires nothing: ADR-020 stays the only mutation path. | `modules/agent/line-binding-status.js`, `modules/agent/phase1-runtime.js`, `modules/line-crm/LineCrmMultiOa.jsx` | `integration/fr146-line-oa-account.test.js`, `unit/line-binding-status.test.js`, `unit/line-oa-settings-consolidation.test.js`, `unit/phase1-business-agent-runtime.test.js` | ✅ |

| FR-149 | Server-owned LINE conversation transport — native signed webhooks resolve an explicitly enabled CLOUD account, record each event as Integration evidence and acknowledge LINE on that durable capture, admit it asynchronously into inbound CRM and a uniquely keyed conversation job in one transaction, and use the Integration-owned LINE port to send and record provider acceptance. An admission abandoned by process exit is not lost: the evidence row is labelled `ADMITTING` before the first attempt, and the bounded worker tick re-admits rows stranded there from the stored payload. A reconciled event carries no reply token — evidence strips it, and LINE's token is dead long before a reconciler runs — so it is delivered by Push where the account permits delayed Push, and otherwise ends `FAILED` visibly rather than silently. Terminal `FAILED` jobs are counted with their `errorCode` for the selected Business on the Studio conversation surface, so a customer who received no answer is visible without a database query. One worker tick answers several waiting customers at once and delivers several ready answers in turn, so nobody is queued behind another customer's model call, and the supervisor's polling cadence follows the work — quick while there is any, backing off while there is none, with admission waking it directly when it queues a job. Per-account ownership fencing, immutable send payload, bounded retries, secret redaction and receipt recovery prevent duplicate or falsely reported sends. | | FR-151 | LINE OA rich menu designer — `LineOaRichMenu` and `LineOaRichMenuVersion`, the Studio-owned design record of one rich menu per LINE OA Studio account, many per account (ADR-060 D3; SRS LOS-RQ-040, LOS-RQ-041, LOS-RQ-016). A menu is the identity (`code` unique per Tenant, optional `alias` unique per account, default flag, DRAFT → READY → ARCHIVED); every body is a numbered version carrying the LINE layout (1×1, 2×1, 2×2, 2×3, 3×1, 1×2), chat-bar text (≤ 14 characters), up to twenty tap areas whose actions are an allow-listed vocabulary (message, postback, https/tel URI, LIFF app reference, rich-menu switch by alias) and never a free-form object, the declared image size (one of LINE's six) and a `FileAsset` image reference validated as a same-Business, live PNG/JPEG within LINE's 1 MiB — bytes are never copied (FR-045). Saving edits the open draft in place and reports every reason it could not be deployed (unsupported image size, an area outside the image, no areas, no image); freezing refuses a draft with any such reason and makes the version immutable, so the next save opens the next numbered version; archiving retires the open draft and keeps every frozen version. Writes need Business OWNER or `LINE_OA_PUBLISHER`, reads need Business visibility plus the `line-oa` domain, every refusal is the FR-072 404, every write is a compare-and-swap on the menu's `version` with one audit row. The external `richMenuId` is a nullable attribute of the deployed version (BR-002), written by the transport lane; this requirement never talks to LINE — publishing a frozen version is the | FR-152 | LINE OA rich menu publish jobs, server-owned — `LineOaRichMenuJob`, the durable ledger through which a frozen `LineOaRichMenuVersion` (FR-151) reaches LINE under ADR-061's server-owned transport: a publisher queues one job per menu (`PUBLISH` the newest FROZEN version; `SET_DEFAULT` or `SET_ALIAS` a PUBLISHED one that carries an external `richMenuId`) on an account that is CONNECTED, CLOUD and `serverEnabled`, with the menu's `version` as the compare-and-swap and at most one open job per menu; the server worker (`POST /api/line-oa/rich-menu-worker`, same bearer and bounded-tick contract as FR-149's worker, ticked by the same supervised script) claims the oldest due job with compare-and-set and a bounded lease, fences on the account's status, transport mode, server flag and `transportEpoch` before any external call, resolves the account credential per attempt through the Integration lane's secret mount, and walks the job's stages through the Integration-owned rich menu port (create → upload image → done; or apply). Acceptance is the provider's HTTP acceptance, never a delivery claim: on it the version becomes PUBLISHED with its `richMenuId` and the previously PUBLISHED version RETIRED, or the account's single default moves, or the alias is set. Idempotency decides ambiguity — an unconfirmed create (timeout, unreadable id) ends `UNKNOWN`, visible to a publisher who acknowledges it and never retried because a second create would duplicate the menu; an unconfirmed or 5xx upload, default or alias returns to QUEUED with exponential backoff and reuses the persisted id; a 4xx is FAILED with the id kept for cleanup; an expired lease is UNKNOWN. `LIFF` actions have no URL until the LIFF registry exists and are refused at queue time. | FR-153 | LINE OA LIFF app registry — `LineOaLiffApp`, the per-account record of the LIFF apps a LINE Official Account exposes (SRS LOS-RQ-070): `code` unique per Tenant, name, description, view size (COMPACT / TALL / FULL), an https endpoint, an allow-listed scope set (profile, openid, email, chat_message.write), a bot-prompt mode, and the external `liffId` LINE Developers issued — validated as a shape, unique per account, recorded by a publisher as an attribute and never looked up (BR-002). A registration is DRAFT until its `liffId` is recorded (`RECORD_LIFF_ID` activates it), ACTIVE from then, ARCHIVED on request; `UPDATE` edits the fields; every write needs Business OWNER or `LINE_OA_PUBLISHER`, every read Business visibility plus the `line-oa` domain, every refusal is the FR-072 404, every write a compare-and-swap on `version` with one audit row. The registry's purpose is resolution: a rich menu `LIFF` tap action (FR-151) names an app by code and publishes (FR-152) as `https://liff.line.me/{liffId}{path}` through an ACTIVE app — resolved at queue time and again at execution — and is refused by code when the account has no such app (`LIFF_UNRESOLVED`) or it is not active (`LIFF_NOT_ACTIVE`), never invented. | FR-155 | Inventory stock ledger — for a TRACKED product (FR-154), an append-only `StockMovement` (RECEIPT adds, ISSUE removes, ADJUSTMENT carries its own sign, with `reason`, `reference`, `actorId`, `occurredAt`); on-hand is the sum of a product's rows recomputed on every read and never stored on the product, a summary flags on-hand below `safetyStock`, and an UNTRACKED product is refused by code (`INVENTORY_PRODUCT_UNTRACKED`) and reports `null` on-hand, never zero. `ProductLot` (`lot_id`: `code` unique per product, optional factory, manufacture and expiry dates, `receivedQty`, OPEN / QUARANTINE / CLOSED) — a LOT-tracked receipt names a lot by id or creates it by code and the lot's `receivedQty` follows; a receipt into a CLOSED lot is refused. `SerialUnit` (`serial_id`: `serialNo` unique per product, IN_STOCK / RESERVED / ISSUED / RETURNED / SCRAPPED) — a SERIAL-tracked movement names exactly one serial per unit and writes one ledger row per unit; a unit is created IN_STOCK by its receipt, ISSUED by its issue, refused when received while IN_STOCK or issued while not, and ADJUSTMENT is not allowed on a serial product. A plain (NONE) product refuses a lot or a serial on the movement; an ISSUE that would take on-hand below zero is refused. Serial units are created only through the ledger (`GET`-only route); an explicit lot route exists for a dated batch before stock arrives. An ISSUE of a LOT-tracked SKU that names no lot is consumed **FEFO** — first expired, first out — across OPEN lots (unknown expiry last, whatever no lot holds last), one ledger row per lot touched and the allocation in the audit payload; an ISSUE that names a lot may not exceed that lot's on-hand (`INVENTORY_LOT_INSUFFICIENT_STOCK`); a lot listing reports each lot's on-hand from the ledger. Manager authority, FR-072 refusals, one transaction and one audit row per write with `onHandBefore` / `onHandAfter` in the payload. | `app/(pm)/inventory/page.jsx`, `app/api/inventory/lots/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/serial-units/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/stock-movements/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/stock/route.js`, `modules/inventory/application/inventory-stock-service.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/inventory.js`, `modules/inventory/index.js`, `modules/project-manager/api-docs/openapi.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/backup-service.js` | `e2e/fr154-inventory-dashboard.spec.js`, `integration/backup.test.js`, `integration/crm-conversation-analysis.test.js`, `integration/fr075-restore-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr155-inventory-stock.test.js`, `integration/fr165-goods-receipt.test.js`, `integration/fr176-customization-work-order.test.js`, `integration/fr177-kitting-work-order.test.js`, `integration/fr178-de-kitting.test.js`, `integration/fr184-inventory-stocktake.test.js`, `integration/line-server-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-content-backup.test.js`, `integration/openapi-docs.test.js`, `unit/domain-state.test.js`, `unit/fr045-backup-contract.test.js`, `unit/inventory-domain.test.js`, `unit/inventory-routes.test.js`, `unit/procurement-routes.test.js`, `unit/scm-console-routes.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-156 | Inventory recipe / bill of materials at a batch size — `ProductRecipe` (`recipe_id`: `code` unique per Tenant, one row per output SKU and `batchSize` — "the recipe for 10 seats" and "for 20 seats" are two rows on one product, exactly as a gift box has a BOM at 10 / 50 / 100 / 500 sets — with `yieldQty`, `unit`, `notes`, ACTIVE / ARCHIVED, `version`) and `ProductRecipeLine` (a same-Business component SKU with `qty` per batch, optional `unit`, `fixed` for a line that does not scale with the quantity built, such as tooling; a component appears once and is never the output). Pure calculators pick the recipe for a quantity (the largest batch size that fits, else the smallest), explode it (scaled lines multiply by `quantity / batchSize`, fixed lines do not, the output yield follows the batch), compare with on-hand recomputed from the ledger (a fractional requirement is issued as the next whole unit; an uncounted component never blocks) and state the maximum buildable quantity. `GET /api/inventory/recipes/[id]?quantity=` returns that explosion; `POST …/build` is one transaction that issues every counted component through the FR-155 ledger (FEFO for lots) and receives the output SKU when it is counted — refused whole with `INVENTORY_RECIPE_SHORTAGE` and a per-component list when any component is short, and refused when a component or the output is SERIAL-tracked or a LOT-tracked output has no `outputLotCode`. `UPDATE` replaces the fields and, when given, the whole line set; `ARCHIVE` keeps the row; both compare-and-swap on `version`; manager authority, FR-072 refusals, one audit row per write. A bundle (FR-154) is the special case "a kit at batch size 1"; offers and price tiers stay in Commerce. | `app/api/inventory/recipes/[id]/build/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/recipes/[id]/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/recipes/route.js`, `modules/inventory/application/inventory-recipe-service.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/inventory.js`, `modules/project-manager/api-docs/openapi.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/backup-service.js` | `integration/backup.test.js`, `integration/crm-conversation-analysis.test.js`, `integration/fr075-restore-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr156-inventory-recipe.test.js`, `integration/fr177-kitting-work-order.test.js`, `integration/fr178-de-kitting.test.js`, `integration/line-server-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-content-backup.test.js`, `integration/openapi-docs.test.js`, `unit/fr045-backup-contract.test.js`, `unit/inventory-domain.test.js`, `unit/inventory-routes.test.js` | ✅ |

| FR-161 | Sales tasks (crm) — `SalesTask`, the follow-up a salesperson owes a customer (ADR-064: the legacy ERD's "Tasks" adapted as a *sales* activity, deliberately not a project-manager `WorkItem`): Business-scoped, identity an internal UUID with a generated human `code` `TSK-YYYYMMDD-NNN` unique per Tenant (BR-002); optional `customerId` and `conversationId` of the same Tenant reached through the Business's tenant only and visible to the viewer (BR-001), a Conversation supplying its Customer when none is named and refusing a different one; optional `assigneePersonId` that must hold an ACTIVE Membership covering the Business; `type` (FOLLOW_UP / CALL / LINE_MESSAGE / EMAIL / MEETING / DEMO / QUOTE), `priority` (URGENT / HIGH / NORMAL / LOW — the legacy URGENT status folded here), `scheduleKind` SINGLE (one `dueDate`, optional `timeStart` / `timeEnd` window) or RANGE (`startDate` → `dueDate`), `description`, `outcome`, `cancelReason`. Status machine OPEN → IN_PROGRESS → DONE, OPEN or IN_PROGRESS → CANCELLED, DONE or CANCELLED → OPEN only via REOPEN; UPDATE and ASSIGN refused on a closed task; every action compare-and-swap on `version` with one audit row; nothing deleted. Due state (OVERDUE / TODAY / UPCOMING) and the summary (open, in progress, overdue, due today, mine, unassigned) are computed on read against the Business calendar (Asia/Bangkok), never stored. Reads need Business visibility plus the `customer` domain (FR-061; the FR-072 404 without it); writes need Business OWNER or `SALES_REP` (`crm.sales-task.write`, FR-076 pattern; an honest 403 for a member holding the domain). | FR-163 | Payments, verification and revenue (commerce) — `Payment`, a PAYMENT or REFUND against a sales order (ADR-065): `code` `PAY-YYYYMMDD-NNN` unique per Tenant, `method` (TRANSFER / CASH / QR / CARD / OTHER), an exact `amountSatang`, an optional `bankReference` that is an attribute unique per Tenant (the legacy duplicate-slip rule kept, the key refused — BR-002, ADR-054 D4), an optional slip that must be a `FileAsset` of the same Business, `paidAt`; PENDING until a verifier confirms (VERIFIED) or rejects it with a reason (REJECTED); only a PENDING payment can be acted on. Recording needs Business OWNER or `SALES_REP`; verifying or rejecting needs Business OWNER or `PAYMENT_VERIFIER` (`commerce.payment.verify`), because verified money is what everything else is counted from: the order's paid, balance due and payment state (UNPAID / PARTIAL / PAID / OVERPAID / REFUNDED — derived, never a column) follow VERIFIED money only; a refund is never verified beyond what was verifiably paid; a payment cannot be recorded on a CANCELLED order, a refund can. `GET /api/commerce/revenue` counts VERIFIED payments net of VERIFIED refunds on the day the money was paid in the Business's calendar (Asia/Bangkok), by origin (CHAT / WALK_IN / ONLINE) and by day, with pending money beside it — the legacy "ROAS from VERIFIED only" kept; no ad attribution until an Ad model exists. Slip OCR is not verification and is not called. | FR-164 | Suppliers and purchase orders (procurement) — `Supplier`, `PurchaseOrder` and `PurchaseOrderLine`, the buy side of a Business (ADR-066: the legacy ERD's Phase 5 procurement shapes adapted, corrections recorded): a Supplier is Business-scoped with a human `code` unique per Tenant as an attribute (BR-002), contact, tax id, payment terms and lead time as plain columns, ACTIVE until `ARCHIVE` keeps the row (an archived supplier takes no new order, `SUPPLIER_ARCHIVED`); a purchase order has a generated `code` `PO-YYYYMMDD-NNN` unique per Tenant, names an ACTIVE supplier of its own Business (`SUPPLIER_NOT_FOUND` otherwise) and carries lines (never a JSON blob) that may name a non-archived Inventory `Product` of the same Business at the unit cost agreed for this purchase — integer satang in every column, baht with at most two decimals in the API. Total, received value, outstanding value, each line's received and outstanding quantity and the order's `receiptState` (NONE or PARTIAL or COMPLETE) are computed on every read from the lines and their receipt lines, never stored. Status machine DRAFT → SENT (`SEND`), SENT → RECEIVED only by the goods receipt that completes every line (FR-165), SENT → SHORT_CLOSED (`CLOSE`, the short-close with lines outstanding and a reason), DRAFT or SENT → CANCELLED (`CANCEL`, refused once a receipt exists, `PURCHASE_ORDER_HAS_RECEIPTS`); `UPDATE` replaces lines and the supplier only while DRAFT (`PURCHASE_ORDER_LINES_LOCKED`, `PURCHASE_ORDER_SUPPLIER_LOCKED` after SEND), notes and the expected date while open. Compare-and-swap on `version`, one audit row per write, nothing deleted. Reads need Business visibility plus the `procurement` domain (FR-061; the FR-072 404 without it); writes need Business OWNER or `PROCUREMENT_BUYER` (`procurement.po.write`, FR-076 pattern). | | FR-165 | Goods receipts into the stock ledger (procurement) — `GoodsReceipt` and `GoodsReceiptLine` (ADR-066 D4/D6): a receipt with a generated `code` `GRN-YYYYMMDD-NNN` unique per Tenant, the supplier's delivery-note number as an attribute and `receivedAt`, posted only against a SENT purchase order (`PURCHASE_ORDER_NOT_RECEIVABLE` otherwise); every receipt line names one line of that order (`PROCUREMENT_RECEIPT_LINE_NOT_FOUND`, once per receipt) and may not receive more than the line still has outstanding — refused whole with the per-line list (`PROCUREMENT_RECEIPT_EXCEEDS_ORDERED`). A line whose order line names a counted (TRACKED) SKU writes RECEIPT rows into the Inventory ledger through Inventory's exported `appendMovement` inside the same transaction with reason `GOODS_RECEIPT` and reference `PO:<code>/GRN:<code>` — `lotCode` names or creates the lot (a LOT-tracked SKU needs one; Inventory's `INVENTORY_LOT_REQUIRED` bubbles up), a given `expiresAt` is set on a lot that has no expiry yet and never overwrites one, `serialNos` create the units (exactly one per unit; `INVENTORY_SERIAL_COUNT_MISMATCH` bubbles up); an uncounted SKU or a free-text line is recorded on the receipt, touches no ledger and refuses lot or serial data (`PROCUREMENT_RECEIPT_LINE_NOT_COUNTED`). The ledger half needs Inventory's write authority on top of the buyer's (`403 PROCUREMENT_RECEIPT_REQUIRES_INVENTORY_AUTHORITY`; a Procurement role never widens Inventory); a receipt of only uncounted or free-text lines needs none. The receipt that completes every line makes the order RECEIVED in the same transaction (audited on the order too); the order's `version` moves with each receipt. A receipt has no status, no version and no PATCH — never edited or deleted; a wrong one is corrected by an Inventory ADJUSTMENT. Posting needs Business OWNER or `PROCUREMENT_BUYER` (`procurement.receipt.post`); reading needs the `procurement` domain; every refusal of scope is the FR-072 404; one audit row per receipt. | `app/(pm)/procurement/page.jsx`, `app/(pm)/procurement/purchase-orders/page.jsx`, `app/(pm)/procurement/receipts/page.jsx`, `app/api/procurement/purchase-orders/[id]/receipts/route.js`, `app/api/procurement/receipts/[id]/route.js`, `app/api/procurement/receipts/route.js`, `config/domains.js`, `lib/validation/enums.js`, `modules/procurement/application/goods-receipt-service.js`, `modules/procurement/application/procurement-authority.js`, `modules/procurement/domain/procurement.js`, `modules/procurement/index.js`, `modules/project-manager/api-docs/openapi.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/backup-service.js` | `e2e/fr041-business-first.spec.js`, `e2e/fr164-procurement.spec.js`, `e2e/fr165-receipt-workstation.spec.js`, `integration/backup.test.js`, `integration/crm-conversation-analysis.test.js`, `integration/fr075-restore-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr164-procurement.test.js`, `integration/fr165-goods-receipt.test.js`, `integration/line-server-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-content-backup.test.js`, `integration/openapi-docs.test.js`, `integration/xlsx-intake.test.js`, `unit/asset-management-navigation.test.js`, `unit/business-capability-navigation.test.js`, `unit/conversation-analysis-contract.test.js`, `unit/crm-group-navigation.test.js`, `unit/domain-navigation.test.js`, `unit/domain-state.test.js`, `unit/fr045-api-ui-contract.test.js`, `unit/fr045-backup-contract.test.js`, `unit/inventory-routes.test.js`, `unit/marketing-navigation.test.js`, `unit/plan-schema.test.js`, `unit/procurement-domain.test.js`, `unit/procurement-routes.test.js`, `unit/scm-group-navigation.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-166 | Sales orders (commerce) — `SalesOrder` and `SalesOrderLine`, what a Business sold (ADR-065: the legacy ERD's Orders section adapted, corrections recorded): Business-scoped, identity an internal UUID with a generated human `code` `ORD-YYYYMMDD-NNN` unique per Tenant (BR-002); optional `customerId` and `conversationId` of the same Tenant reached through the Business's tenant only (BR-001) — a Conversation supplies its Customer, refuses a different one, and makes the `origin` CHAT (the legacy "ads revenue"); otherwise `origin` WALK_IN or ONLINE. Lines (never a JSON blob) may name an ACTIVE Inventory `Product` of the same Business and carry the price given at the time of sale, a quantity and a discount; money is integer satang in every column and baht with at most two decimals in the API. Subtotal, total, paid, balance due and the payment state are computed on every read from the lines and the VERIFIED payments, never stored. Status machine DRAFT → CONFIRMED → COMPLETED, DRAFT or CONFIRMED → CANCELLED; `UPDATE` replaces lines only while DRAFT (`SALES_ORDER_LINES_LOCKED` after CONFIRM), notes and discount while open; `COMPLETE` may issue stock — every line naming a counted SKU is issued through Inventory's exported `appendMovement` inside the order's transaction (FEFO for lots, reference `ORDER:<code>`), refused whole with a per-SKU list when any is short, refused for a SERIAL-tracked line, and refused when the viewer lacks Inventory's write authority (a Commerce role never widens Inventory); `CANCEL` keeps the row and its payments. Compare-and-swap on `version`, one audit row per write, nothing deleted. Reads need Business visibility plus the `commerce` domain (FR-061; the FR-072 404 without it); writes need Business OWNER or `SALES_REP` (`commerce.order.write`, FR-076 pattern). | `app/(pm)/commerce/orders/page.jsx`, `app/(pm)/commerce/page.jsx`, `app/api/commerce/orders/[id]/route.js`, `app/api/commerce/orders/route.js`, `config/domains.js`, `modules/commerce/application/commerce-authority.js`, `modules/commerce/application/sales-order-service.js`, `modules/commerce/domain/commerce.js`, `modules/commerce/index.js`, `modules/identity/rbac.js`, `modules/project-manager/api-docs/openapi.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/backup-service.js` | `e2e/fr041-business-first.spec.js`, `e2e/fr166-commerce-orders.spec.js`, `e2e/navigation-reachability.spec.js`, `integration/backup.test.js`, `integration/crm-conversation-analysis.test.js`, `integration/fr075-restore-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr163-payment.test.js`, `integration/fr166-sales-order.test.js`, `integration/line-server-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-content-backup.test.js`, `integration/openapi-docs.test.js`, `unit/asset-management-navigation.test.js`, `unit/business-capability-navigation.test.js`, `unit/command-palette-index.test.js`, `unit/commerce-domain.test.js`, `unit/commerce-routes.test.js`, `unit/crm-group-navigation.test.js`, `unit/customer-import-review-service.test.js`, `unit/domain-navigation.test.js`, `unit/domain-state.test.js`, `unit/fr045-api-ui-contract.test.js`, `unit/fr045-backup-contract.test.js`, `unit/fr076-product-owner-business-assignment.test.js`, `unit/inventory-routes.test.js`, `unit/marketing-navigation.test.js`, `unit/procurement-routes.test.js`, `unit/scm-group-navigation.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-167 | Supply-chain navigation groups its four modules under one SCM slot (ADR-069) — `DOMAIN_GROUPS` declares `scm` over the existing `inventory`, `warehouse`, `procurement` and `commerce` domains, the bar renders one slot for the group and the sidebar lists the whole group with each child's label heading its own pages, so a child reached from the bar can reach its siblings. The group is a container and never a grant: `scm` is absent from the registry, so it cannot reach `Membership.domainKeysJson`, the permission checkboxes or the route guard, which keeps resolving a path to the leaf domain and asking about that key (FR-061 unchanged). | FR-168 | A product declares one of three natures, and they differ in accounting rather than in bookkeeping convenience — `stockPolicy` is TRACKED (สินค้านับสต๊อก: a good the Business counts, every movement a ledger row, cost held in stock until the goods leave), UNTRACKED (สินค้าไม่นับสต๊อก: still a good, still buyable and receivable, but carrying no perpetual count, so no on-hand at all rather than a zero) or SERVICE (บริการ: not a good, so no stock fields exist, `movementRule` refuses it by its own code `INVENTORY_PRODUCT_IS_A_SERVICE`, and a goods receipt naming it is refused with `PROCUREMENT_RECEIPT_LINE_IS_A_SERVICE` because a service is performed, not delivered). Anything without a ledger is stored with `trackingMode` NONE, so neither an uncounted good nor a service can ask for lot or serial identity it can never have. The catalogue form narrows to the nature chosen: picking ไม่นับสต๊อก or บริการ removes the tracking mode and safety-stock fields instead of leaving fields that cannot apply. | `app/(pm)/inventory/page.jsx`, `modules/inventory/application/inventory-catalog-service.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/inventory.js`, `modules/procurement/application/goods-receipt-service.js` | `integration/fr154-inventory-catalog.test.js`, `integration/fr165-goods-receipt.test.js`, `unit/inventory-domain.test.js`, `unit/inventory-routes.test.js`, `unit/scm-console-routes.test.js` | ✅ |

| FR-175 | Inventory valuation is landed cost in integer satang, and domestic single-drop freight is absorbed into it rather than quoted — `StockMovement.costSatang` is the unit landed cost of that row (THB × 100, never a float, the ADR-065 D2 money rule). `landedUnitCostSatang = factoryCostSatang + ceil(seaFreight/batch) + ceil(duty/batch) + ceil(inboundTruck/batch) + customizationPerUnit + kittingPerUnit`, each shared cost divided by the batch and rounded up to the satang so a batch is never valued below what it cost. The inbound truck defaults to the flat 250,000 satang (2,500 THB) single-drop rate, which is why a standard quote shows delivery as 0.00 and must carry no separate freight line — a second drop or an island crossing is a different service and belongs to Commerce. Valuation across receipts is moving weighted average, recomputed from the ledger like every other inventory number. | `modules/inventory/application/inventory-stock-service.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/inventory-costing.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/inventory.js`, `modules/inventory/index.js` | `integration/fr155-inventory-stock.test.js`, `integration/fr176-customization-work-order.test.js`, `integration/fr177-kitting-work-order.test.js`, `integration/fr184-inventory-stocktake.test.js`, `unit/inventory-costing.test.js`, `unit/inventory-domain.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-176 | A customization work order is the durable record of branded work in progress, and branding is irreversible — `CustomizationWorkOrder` (`CWO-YYYYMMDD-NNN`) binds one raw SKU, one technique (`LASER_ENGRAVING`, `SILK_SCREEN`, `UV_DIGITAL_PRINT`, `HOT_STAMP_FOIL`, `EMBOSSING`), one customer and one sales order, with artwork, Pantone colours, setup and run cost in satang, and issued / completed / scrapped quantities as it runs (`DRAFT → RELEASED → IN_PROGRESS → COMPLETED \| CANCELLED`, plus `BLOCKED_SHORTAGE` when scrap outruns the buffer). Releasing it transfers the gross quantity into `TH_WIP_CUSTOMIZATION`; completing it consumes the raw units, issues scrap to `TH_QUARANTINE_SCRAP` and receives a different product — `itemKind` `CUSTOM_COMPONENT` with `dedicatedCustomerId` / `dedicatedSalesOrderId` set — which the domain then refuses to issue to another customer, to transfer back into generic raw stock, or to consume for another order. | FR-177 | A kitting work order assembles a finished gift set from a recipe with a declared scrap allowance — `KittingWorkOrder` (`KWO-YYYYMMDD-NNN`) explodes one `ProductRecipe` for a planned quantity, and `ProductRecipe.scrapAllowanceFactor` (a fraction in `[0, 0.20]`, default 0, so every recipe written before this requirement explodes exactly as it did) makes the gross issue `ceil(net × (1 + factor))` — the buffer is issued up front rather than fetched mid-line. Completion consumes the components, records assembled and scrapped quantities, blends the component landed costs plus packaging and assembly labour into the finished unit's `costSatang`, and receives the set at `TH_FINISHED_GOODS`. The output must carry a FlowAccount item code matching `^[A-Z0-9]+-[0-9]+\([A-Z0-9_-]+\)$` (model, item count, package code — `TMS06-4(P-16)`), and a run without one is refused. That code is deliberately **not** `Product.code` — FR-154's code pattern has no room for parentheses, and widening it for one integration would be the wrong reading — and equally deliberately not an `ExternalRef` row, which is unique on `(system, value)` installation-wide and carries no `tenantId`, so two Tenants importing from the same factory could not both name their four-item set `TMS06-4(P-16)`. It is one nullable `Product.flowAccountSku` column, unique per `(tenantId, flowAccountSku)`, an attribute and never a key (BR-002), written only by `createProduct` and `setFlowAccountSku`, both of which validate the pattern. Validated where a set is produced rather than where a product is named, because components and packaging are never synchronised to FlowAccount at all. | `app/(pm)/inventory/work-orders/page.jsx`, `app/api/inventory/kitting-work-orders/[id]/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/kitting-work-orders/route.js`, `modules/inventory/application/kitting-work-order-service.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/inventory-costing.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/inventory-wip.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/inventory.js`, `modules/inventory/index.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/backup-service.js` | `integration/backup.test.js`, `integration/crm-conversation-analysis.test.js`, `integration/fr075-restore-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr177-kitting-work-order.test.js`, `integration/fr182-scm-console-actions.test.js`, `integration/line-server-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-content-backup.test.js`, `unit/fr045-backup-contract.test.js`, `unit/inventory-costing.test.js`, `unit/inventory-domain.test.js`, `unit/inventory-wip.test.js`, `unit/scm-console-routes.test.js` | ✅ |

| FR-179 | A stored unit that ages and can be restored by an operation is guarded on issue, not merely reported — `Product.maintenanceIntervalDays` and `Product.maxStorageDays` (both nullable, so a product with neither behaves exactly as before) and `ProductLot.lastMaintainedAt` make a lot's storage age `now − (lastMaintainedAt ?? manufacturedAt)`. At the maintenance interval the lot is **due**: surfaced as a task, never blocked. Past `maxStorageDays` it is **refused** for issue and for kitting until a maintenance is recorded, which resets the clock. The generalisation past "battery" is deliberate — the rule is about a unit that degrades in storage and is restored by an operation, which is as true of a lithium cell needing a 180-day recharge as of a calibrated instrument. FEFO (FR-155) still chooses which lot goes first; this says which lots may go at all. | `app/api/inventory/shelf-life/route.js`, `modules/inventory/application/inventory-catalog-service.js`, `modules/inventory/application/inventory-shelf-life-service.js`, `modules/inventory/application/inventory-stock-service.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/inventory-wip.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/inventory.js`, `modules/inventory/index.js` | `integration/fr154-inventory-catalog.test.js`, `integration/fr155-inventory-stock.test.js`, `integration/fr179-shelf-life-guard.test.js`, `integration/fr182-scm-console-actions.test.js`, `integration/fr184-inventory-stocktake.test.js`, `unit/inventory-domain.test.js`, `unit/inventory-wip.test.js`, `unit/scm-console-routes.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-180 | Available-to-Promise subtracts what is already spoken for, in two tiers — `StockReservation` holds one product's quantity for one purpose: `QUOTE` (soft, expiring, 7 days by default) or `ORDER` (committed against a confirmed sales order), so `ATP = onHand − Σ committed − Σ live quote reservations`. A reservation is never deleted: it moves to `RELEASED`, `CONVERTED` or `EXPIRED`, so "why could we not promise those 500 on Tuesday" stays answerable. Expiry is evaluated on read against the clock rather than by a sweeper, so an ATP figure is correct even when no worker has run. Reservations never write the stock ledger — a reservation is a promise and the ledger is physical fact — and the maximum buildable set count is computed from ATP rather than on-hand, so two quotes cannot both promise the same tumblers. | `app/(pm)/inventory/reservations/page.jsx`, `app/api/inventory/atp/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/reservations/[id]/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/reservations/route.js`, `modules/inventory/application/inventory-atp-service.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/inventory-wip.js`, `modules/inventory/index.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/backup-service.js` | `integration/backup.test.js`, `integration/crm-conversation-analysis.test.js`, `integration/fr075-restore-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr177-kitting-work-order.test.js`, `integration/fr180-atp-reservations.test.js`, `integration/fr182-scm-console-actions.test.js`, `integration/line-server-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-content-backup.test.js`, `unit/fr045-backup-contract.test.js`, `unit/inventory-domain.test.js`, `unit/inventory-wip.test.js`, `unit/scm-console-routes.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-181 | The agent reaches the supply chain through six tools on the gates it already has, and through no other path — three read tools in the Gate E registry (`check_inventory_atp`, `calculate_smartgift_quote`, `audit_battery_lots`) and three write actions in the Gate F registry (`create_quote_stock_reservation` at LOW sensitivity; `dispatch_customization_work_order` and `dispatch_kitting_work_order` at HIGH, so the action gate demands step-up before stock is irreversibly branded or consumed). Every tool calls the same application service a human surface calls, with the same viewer and therefore the same authority ladder and the same FR-072 refusals; none queries Prisma directly and none carries a Business the caller did not already prove. `calculate_smartgift_quote` states the absorbed freight explicitly (`freightSatang: 0`, `freightAbsorbedSatang: 250000`) so a quote can never present it as a separate charge (FR-175). The Business's own system prompts are its agent configuration, not zuri-ai code; this requirement ships the tools they call. | `modules/agent/tools/smartgift-inventory-tools.js` | `integration/fr181-smartgift-agent-tools.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-182 | The SCM lane gets the human surface ADR-074 deferred: `/api/inventory/**` handlers and the console pages for locations, transfers, the two work orders, reservations and the shelf-life audit. Thirteen route files, each thin — resolve the viewer, call the one exported service, return what it returns — so every authority rule, refusal code and audit row is the service's and none is re-implemented at the edge; a route never touches Prisma. Two of them are new **action dispatchers** (`PATCH /api/inventory/customization-work-orders/{id}` and `.../kitting-work-orders/{id}`) whose `action` — RELEASE \| COMPLETE \| CANCEL — is a Zod-validated vocabulary in the domain, not a string a handler branches on, so the work-order verbs are declared in one place and a route cannot invent a fourth. Four pages: `/inventory` keeps the catalogue and ledger it already had, `/inventory/locations` shows on-hand per location beside the Business-wide total (BR-026) and moves stock, `/inventory/work-orders` opens, releases and completes both kinds, and `/inventory/reservations` shows ATP with its two tiers and holds or releases stock. | FR-186 | Business-scoped durable Commerce document issuance and issuer/tax/PromptPay configuration — `CommerceDocument` is an immutable snapshot linked to one Business, sales order and selected active Branch, with closed types `INVOICE`, `RECEIPT`, `TAX_INVOICE` and `ABB_TAX_INVOICE`. `POST /api/commerce/billing/documents/preview` validates scope, configured issuer/tax/recipient policy and buyer `ISSUANCE_INPUT` without persisting a number, issued timestamp or audit event; `POST /api/commerce/billing/documents` performs transactional issuance with one immutable snapshot, one audit event, a retry-safe idempotency key and an atomic per-Business/type/calendar-year sequence. Seller identity comes from the Business's LegalEntity, verified Thai identifier and configured legal/Branch address; a Business OWNER edits issuer, versioned VAT, PromptPay, non-VAT and walk-in policy through the settings flow, and missing/inactive/unverified values remain explicitly unavailable. VAT issue requires the configured effective, verified policy; durable `RECEIPT` issue requires verified payment at least equal to document gross, while preview may show unpaid or pending state without claiming receipt. P3 uses document-level `ROUND_HALF_UP` integer-satang VAT, requires `nonVatDocumentPolicy = ALLOW_INVOICE_RECEIPT` for non-VAT invoice/receipt and `walkInDocumentPolicy = ALLOW_ANONYMOUS_RECEIPT` for receipt-only anonymous walk-in; full tax documents require validated buyer issuance input. Billing accepts only the existing FR-166 `THB` order currency in this slice; unsupported currencies fail before preview or issue with no FX conversion. PromptPay is the existing `Payment.method = QR` with a Business-scoped configured, active, verified recipient; the local EMVCo/Thai QR payload is not bank or statutory evidence. No void/correction route, CRM write, terminal registry, price catalogue, live provider call or production activation is part of this requirement. | `app/(pm)/commerce/invoices/page.jsx`, `app/api/commerce/billing/config/route.js`, `app/api/commerce/billing/documents/[id]/route.js`, `app/api/commerce/billing/documents/preview/route.js`, `app/api/commerce/billing/documents/route.js`, `modules/commerce/application/billing-invoice-service.js`, `modules/commerce/components/BillingWorkspace.jsx`, `modules/commerce/components/PaymentQR.jsx`, `modules/commerce/domain/billing.js`, `modules/project-manager/api-docs/openapi.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/backup-service.js` | `e2e/fr186-billing-pos.spec.js`, `integration/backup.test.js`, `integration/crm-conversation-analysis.test.js`, `integration/fr075-restore-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr183-pos.test.js`, `integration/fr186-billing.test.js`, `integration/line-server-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-content-backup.test.js`, `integration/openapi-docs.test.js`, `unit/commerce-billing-domain.test.js`, `unit/fr045-backup-contract.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-187 | A structured-record source adapter enters the 17-stage knowledge ingestion pipeline before Stage 1 for SmartGift catalog data (ADR-075 D2, proposed) — SmartGift's 5-stage ETL becomes a source producer: its prepared JSON projection plus its own SHA-256-versioned registry is the version identity the adapter authorizes, freezes and enqueues into `DPL-KNOWLEDGE-INGEST-V1`, never synthesizing any stage result (extension map, `KNOWLEDGE-INGESTION-17-STAGE-FLOW.md` "Before Stage 1" row). Classification at Stage 5 (`DPS-KI-CLASSIFY`) enforces Zero-PII with the same deny policy SmartGift already runs (`pipeline/knowledge_registry/core.py` `DENIED` regex), so a customer/contact/quotation record can never be admitted as a product occurrence. SKU / FlowAccount codes travel as an attribute of the occurrence, never as its `resolutionKey`, and resolve through the existing per-Tenant `Product.flowAccountSku` (FR-174…FR-181 / ADR-074 D5) rather than a new catalog-vault mapping (BR-002). Replaces SmartGift Stage 5's direct `vaults/vlt-catalog-product/genesis-db` write; opens no Stage 18. | `app/api/knowledge/ingestions/route.js`, `modules/knowledge/knowledge-admission-service.js`, `modules/knowledge/knowledge-runtime.js`, `modules/knowledge/smartgift-catalog-adapter.js`, `modules/knowledge/structured-record-policy.js`, `modules/project-manager/mcp/transport.js`, `platform/integrations/core/genesisrag17-executor.js` | `acceptance/genesisrag17-smartgift.test.js`, `acceptance/knowledge-admission-native.test.js`, `integration/genesisrag17-parser-2.test.js`, `integration/genesisrag17-tier1.test.js`, `integration/smartgift-catalog-admission.test.js`, `unit/genesisrag17-parser-2.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-admission-mcp.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-admission-routes.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-admission-service.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-runtime.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-zero-pii-policy.test.js`, `unit/pipeline-mcp-transport.test.js`, `unit/project-manager-mcp.test.js`, `unit/smartgift-catalog-adapter.test.js` | ✅ |

| FR-189 | Edge answers SmartGift catalog queries from the published GenesisRAG17 generation through MSP, with Genesis RAG v4 as a transitional fallback (ADR-075 D7, proposed) — the edge query path becomes source caller → MSP credential/scope check → worker loopback query → one published generation → citation (the existing post-Stage-17 query flow), instead of `apps/edge`'s v4 pipeline re-deriving and serving its own store directly from a sibling checkout's file (`apps/edge/src/rag/v4/paths.ts` `defaultUpstream`) with no MSP/GKS mediation. v4 remains available only as an explicit, time-boxed fallback during the Phase 4 shadow-then-cutover and is sunset on an owner-set date; it is never a second production write path into GenesisBlockDB. | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
| FR-190 | Server-owned LINE transport reports its own reachability, so a silent channel is visible instead of inferred — for every LineOaAccount with serverEnabled the server derives the age of the last inbound delivery it recorded (from existing Integration evidence and LineConversationJob rows; no new column, no migration) and classifies it OK, QUIET or SILENT against configurable thresholds, and separately asks LINE for the channel configured webhook endpoint and compares it with this deployment own account route, classifying MATCHED, MISMATCHED, DISABLED or UNKNOWN. GET /api/line-oa/accounts/{id}/transport-health answers states, timestamps and durations only — never channel credentials and never the other endpoint contents — the LINE OA console shows the state beside the account, and the existing server worker tick logs one structured warning per account while a state is not OK. Read-only: no writes, no new process, and no automatic change to provider configuration. | `app/api/line-oa/accounts/[id]/transport-health/route.js`, `app/api/line-oa/worker/route.js`, `modules/line-oa-studio/application/line-transport-health.js`, `modules/line-oa-studio/domain/transport-health.js` | `integration/line-admission-reconciler.test.js`, `integration/openapi-docs.test.js`, `integration/server-line-jobs.test.js`, `unit/fr190-line-transport-health.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-191 | An access grant can be withdrawn by a named person, for a stated reason, without destroying the evidence that it existed. `Membership` carries its own provenance (`grantedByPersonId`, `grantReason`, `grantSource`, `expiresAt`) and its own end (`revokedAt`, `revokedByPersonId`, `revokeReason`), and identity exposes `suspendMembership`, `reinstateMembership`, `revokeMembership` and `offboardPerson` — each requiring a reason, each cascading to the dependent RoleBinding rows, each refusing 409 LAST_OWNER rather than leaving a Business with no live owner. A revoked grant is never deleted; a partial unique index excludes it so the same scope can be granted again as a new row. `resolveViewer` additionally denies a grant past its `expiresAt`. Authority is `ownsBusiness` for a BUSINESS-scoped grant and `ownsTenant` for a TENANT-scoped one, which makes the broadest grant in the system administrable for the first time. `Membership` is written only from `src/modules/identity/`, enforced by a preflight ratchet. | `app/api/platform/users/memberships/[id]/lifecycle/route.js`, `app/api/platform/users/offboard/route.js`, `lib/validation/enums.js`, `modules/identity/auth-service.js`, `modules/identity/erase-principal.js`, `modules/identity/membership-grant-service.js`, `modules/identity/membership-lifecycle-service.js`, `modules/identity/profile-permission-service.js`, `modules/identity/resolve-viewer.js`, `modules/project-manager/api-docs/openapi.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/project-team-service.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/scope-service.js` | `integration/crm-conversation-analysis.test.js`, `integration/crm-customer-erasure.test.js`, `integration/fr072-workspace-mutation-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr074-scope-creation-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr191-access-grant-lifecycle.test.js`, `integration/identity-erase.test.js`, `integration/openapi-docs.test.js`, `integration/password-reset-flow.test.js`, `integration/scope-and-isolation.test.js`, `integration/server-line-jobs.test.js`, `integration/workspace-onboarding-flow.test.js`, `integration/xlsx-intake.test.js`, `unit/auth-service.test.js`, `unit/conversation-analysis-contract.test.js`, `unit/fr036-team-authorization.test.js`, `unit/fr046-auth-route.test.js`, `unit/fr061-per-business-domain-visibility.test.js`, `unit/fr062-permissions-read-scope.test.js`, `unit/fr076-product-owner-business-assignment.test.js`, `unit/iam-session.test.js`, `unit/membership-grant-service.test.js`, `unit/membership-lifecycle-service.test.js`, `unit/password-reset-service.test.js`, `unit/plan-schema.test.js`, `unit/profile-permission-service.test.js`, `unit/project-team-service.test.js`, `unit/viewer-gate.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-192 | The scope of a grant is a declared value, never the meaning of a null, and no database side effect may change it. `Membership.scopeType` is TENANT or BUSINESS held by a CHECK against `businessId`; `RoleBinding` takes the same grammar (TENANT, BUSINESS, BRANCH), extending ADR-033 D3 rather than superseding it. `ON DELETE RESTRICT` replaces `ON DELETE SET NULL` on `Membership.businessId`, `Workspace.businessId` and `Project.businessId`, because the null that cascade wrote was read by `buildDomainsByBusiness` as every Business in the Tenant — deleting one Business promoted its members to the whole group and its owners to `ownsTenant`. Tenant ancestry becomes a database invariant via `Business UNIQUE (id, tenantId)` and composite foreign keys; one live grant per person per scope becomes two partial unique indexes; and `Membership.role` defaults to MEMBER instead of OWNER. | `lib/validation/enums.js`, `modules/identity/membership-grant-service.js`, `modules/identity/rbac-service.js`, `modules/identity/rbac.js`, `modules/identity/resolve-viewer.js`, `modules/identity/viewer-domains.js` | `integration/fr191-access-grant-lifecycle.test.js`, `integration/workspace-onboarding-flow.test.js`, `integration/xlsx-intake.test.js`, `unit/conversation-analysis-contract.test.js`, `unit/customer-import-review-service.test.js`, `unit/fr061-per-business-domain-visibility.test.js`, `unit/fr076-product-owner-business-assignment.test.js`, `unit/fr192-tenant-role-binding-resolver.test.js`, `unit/membership-grant-service.test.js`, `unit/plan-schema.test.js`, `unit/viewer-gate.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-195 | `AccessInvite` generalises `WorkspaceInvite` to a scoped invitation over all three authority layers: PORTFOLIO (unchanged FR-067 Workspace collaboration, BR-016), TENANT and BUSINESS (a real `Membership` grant on acceptance, created only through `grantBusinessMembership` — the one creation path, ADR-077 D8). Authority to mint is `assertWorkspaceAdminAuthority` at PORTFOLIO scope, `ownsTenant` at TENANT scope, `ownsBusiness` at BUSINESS scope, each refusing 404 the same way an absent scope does; `role: 'OWNER'` is refused 400 at every scope. The Membership binds to the accepting SESSION's `personId`, never resolved from `invitedEmail` — the invited address only routes the link. Keeps WorkspaceInvite's exact token discipline: SHA-256 hash only, one-use atomic acceptance, one generic `INVALID_OR_EXPIRED_INVITE` refusal for every failure mode. `WorkspaceInvite` and `WorkspaceMembership` held zero rows in production on 2026-09-12, which is what makes the table rename a pure DDL change. | `lib/validation/enums.js`, `modules/identity/access-invite-service.js`, `modules/identity/onboarding-service.js`, `modules/identity/workspace-membership-service.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/backup-service.js` | `integration/backup.test.js`, `integration/crm-conversation-analysis.test.js`, `integration/fr075-restore-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr195-access-invite.test.js`, `integration/line-server-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-content-backup.test.js`, `integration/workspace-collaboration-roster.test.js`, `integration/workspace-onboarding-flow.test.js`, `integration/xlsx-intake.test.js`, `unit/conversation-analysis-contract.test.js`, `unit/fr045-backup-contract.test.js`, `unit/onboarding-service.test.js`, `unit/plan-schema.test.js`, `unit/workspace-invite-service.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-196 | Segregation of duties gets a writer, not only a role list. `ROLE_CONFLICTS` in `rbac.js` declares `SALES_REP`/`PAYMENT_VERIFIER` (ADR-065 D4) and `PROCUREMENT_BUYER`/`GOODS_RECEIVER` (ADR-066 D4 as amended by ADR-079 — `PROCUREMENT_BUYER` no longer holds `procurement.receipt.post`); `assignRoleBinding` refuses 409 `ROLE_CONFLICT` when the assignee already holds the other half in the same Tenant, unless the caller is a TENANT owner passing `sodOverride: { reason }`, which lands on `RoleBinding.sodOverrideReason` and in the audit payload. Independently, `applyPaymentAction` refuses 409 `PAYMENT_SELF_VERIFY_FORBIDDEN` when the verifier is the payment's own recorder — including a Business OWNER, who bypasses every other capability check in that file but not this one — unless `selfVerifyAttested: true` is passed, which is recorded in the audit payload as `selfVerified: true`, never silently. `postGoodsReceipt` applies the identical shape against the purchase order's `createdByPersonId` (`GOODS_RECEIPT_SELF_POST_FORBIDDEN`). | `modules/commerce/application/payment-service.js`, `modules/commerce/domain/commerce.js`, `modules/identity/rbac-service.js`, `modules/identity/rbac.js`, `modules/procurement/application/goods-receipt-service.js`, `modules/procurement/domain/procurement.js` | `integration/fr163-payment.test.js`, `integration/fr165-goods-receipt.test.js`, `integration/fr196-segregation-of-duties.test.js`, `integration/fr196-sod-cannot-be-assembled.test.js`, `unit/commerce-domain.test.js`, `unit/customer-import-review-service.test.js`, `unit/fr076-product-owner-business-assignment.test.js`, `unit/procurement-domain.test.js`, `unit/procurement-routes.test.js` | ✅ |
| FR-197 | Operator access is time-boxed, issuable by a standing operator, and its use is recorded. `issueOperatorGrant` requires an existing `isInstallationOperator` caller, a reason and a mandatory `expiresAt` capped at 90 days; a renewal is a fresh `PlatformGrant` row, with any prior ACTIVE grant for that Person superseded (revoked, its own audit event) in the same transaction — `@@unique(personId, capability)` is replaced by a partial unique index scoped to `status = 'ACTIVE'` so the history survives. `hasOperatorGrant` honours `expiresAt` even while the row is still nominally ACTIVE; nothing flips status on a timer. The bootstrap grant is flagged `standing: true` (it predates any operator to issue it); every issued grant is `standing: false`. Reading `/api/audit` and previewing or restoring a backup now route through `assertOperatorAndRecordUse`, which writes one `OPERATOR_ACTION` audit event per successful use — a denied attempt records nothing, because the refusal happens before the write. The restore path proves authority first and records the use INSIDE its transaction, after the re-insert: a record written beforehand is deleted by the wipe it is recording — read as ADR-017 D6 covering operator READS, a deliberate extension argued in ADR-079 rather than an obvious consequence of it. | `app/api/audit/route.js`, `app/api/backup/export/route.js`, `modules/identity/operator-bootstrap.js`, `modules/identity/operator-use.js`, `modules/project-manager/application/backup-service.js` | `integration/backup.test.js`, `integration/crm-conversation-analysis.test.js`, `integration/fr075-restore-authorization.test.js`, `integration/fr197-operator-grant-lifecycle.test.js`, `integration/line-server-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-backup.test.js`, `integration/marketing-content-backup.test.js`, `unit/audit-page.test.js`, `unit/authorization-seam-routes.test.js`, `unit/fr045-backup-contract.test.js`, `unit/operator-bootstrap.test.js` | ✅ |

## Business rules, security rules and design decisions

Anchored by `@spec` in the code that enforces them.

| BR-017 | There is no bare edge. A dependency edge cannot be created without a declared Handoff Contract, and a declared-but-unsatisfied contract does not release its successor. | BR-021 | Knowledge ingestion idempotency is keyed on source identity, source version, content hash and pipeline version together (spec §29): reprocessing the same event never creates a second copy of the same knowledge, while a change in any one of the four is a legitimately new derivation rather than a duplicate — which is what makes re-parsing after a parser or embedding-model upgrade safe to run over the whole corpus. This is the ingestion-time rule; FR-081's `sha256(tenantId, connectionId, entityType, externalId, payloadHash)` re-delivery identity remains the raw-boundary rule beneath it, and BR-009's single-envelope discipline still governs how any surface reaches the pipeline at all. | `modules/knowledge/dedup.js`, `modules/knowledge/ingestion-job.js`, `platform/integrations/core/knowledge-ingestion-executor.js` | `integration/fr109-ingestion-run-identity.test.js`, `integration/fr109-knowledge-ingestion-executor.test.js`, `integration/fr110-knowledge-reporter-receiver.test.js`, `integration/fr119-knowledge-ingestion-quarantine.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-dedup.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-ingestion-job.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-stage-runner.test.js` | ✅ |
| BR-022 | A knowledge object that fails a stage is quarantined with its full failure envelope — `job_id`, `artifact_id`, `stage`, `error_code`, `error_message`, `retry_count`, `first_failed_at`, `last_failed_at`, `pipeline_version` (spec §28) — and classified as retryable, non-retryable or review-required (spec §27). Silent loss is the one outcome forbidden: a corpus that quietly omits what it could not parse reports a completeness it does not have, and every downstream answer inherits that overstatement without a way to detect it. | `modules/knowledge/quarantine.js`, `modules/knowledge/quarantine.js`, `platform/integrations/core/knowledge-ingestion-executor.js`, `platform/integrations/core/knowledge-ingestion-executor.js` | `integration/fr109-knowledge-ingestion-executor.test.js`, `integration/fr110-knowledge-reporter-receiver.test.js`, `integration/fr119-knowledge-ingestion-quarantine.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-published-snapshot-contract.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-quarantine.test.js` | ✅ |

| BR-026 | Stock changes place only through a transfer, and a transfer is one movement pair in one transaction — the issue at the source and the receipt at the target are written together or not at all, both through the FR-155 ledger writer, so a Business-wide on-hand is unchanged by construction while the located buckets move. A movement may name a source, a target, both (a transfer) or neither (a row written before FR-174, or one whose location is genuinely unknown); a located sum is therefore always reported as its own figure beside the Business-wide total, never as a replacement for it. | `app/(pm)/inventory/locations/page.jsx`, `app/api/inventory/location-stock/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/locations/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/stocktakes/commit/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/stocktakes/preview/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/transfers/route.js`, `modules/inventory/application/inventory-stock-service.js`, `modules/inventory/application/inventory-stocktake-service.js`, `modules/inventory/application/location-transfer-service.js`, `modules/inventory/application/warehouse-location-service.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/inventory-stocktake.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/warehouse-location.js` | `integration/fr155-inventory-stock.test.js`, `integration/fr174-warehouse-locations.test.js`, `integration/fr182-scm-console-actions.test.js`, `integration/fr184-inventory-stocktake.test.js`, `unit/inventory-routes.test.js`, `unit/inventory-stocktake-domain.test.js`, `unit/scm-console-routes.test.js`, `unit/warehouse-location.test.js` | ✅ |
| BR-027 | Money in the stock ledger is integer satang and domestic single-drop freight is absorbed into unit valuation, never quoted as a line — `costSatang` on a movement is the unit landed cost in THB × 100; a float never enters. Shared batch costs (sea freight, duty, the flat inbound truck) are divided by the batch quantity and rounded **up** to the satang, so a batch is never valued below what it cost and the rounding gain is never redistributed, because a valuation that depends on iteration order is not reproducible. A standard single-drop delivery consequently appears to the customer as 0.00; a second drop or an island crossing is a distinct service and is charged by the Commerce lane, not by re-opening the valuation. | `app/api/inventory/kitting-work-orders/[id]/route.js`, `modules/agent/tools/smartgift-inventory-tools.js`, `modules/inventory/application/kitting-work-order-service.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/inventory-costing.js` | `integration/fr176-customization-work-order.test.js`, `integration/fr177-kitting-work-order.test.js`, `integration/fr181-smartgift-agent-tools.test.js`, `integration/fr182-scm-console-actions.test.js`, `unit/inventory-costing.test.js`, `unit/scm-console-routes.test.js` | ✅ |
| BR-028 | Customization is irreversible: once raw stock is branded it is a different, customer-dedicated product and can never return to generic stock — a completed customization work order does not receive its input SKU back. It receives a product whose `itemKind` is `CUSTOM_COMPONENT` carrying `dedicatedCustomerId` and `dedicatedSalesOrderId`, and the domain refuses by code to issue that product for another customer, to transfer it into a raw-stock location, or to consume it in a kitting order for another sales order. Disassembly obeys the same rule: a branded component comes back branded or goes to quarantine, never as raw stock. The single exit is an explicit ADJUSTMENT write-off with a stated reason, which is a decision a person makes and the audit records. | `app/(pm)/inventory/work-orders/page.jsx`, `app/api/inventory/customization-work-orders/[id]/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/customization-work-orders/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/de-kitting/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/transfers/route.js`, `modules/inventory/application/customization-work-order-service.js`, `modules/inventory/application/de-kitting-service.js`, `modules/inventory/application/inventory-stock-service.js`, `modules/inventory/application/kitting-work-order-service.js`, `modules/inventory/application/location-transfer-service.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/inventory-wip.js` | `integration/fr155-inventory-stock.test.js`, `integration/fr174-warehouse-locations.test.js`, `integration/fr176-customization-work-order.test.js`, `integration/fr177-kitting-work-order.test.js`, `integration/fr178-de-kitting.test.js`, `integration/fr182-scm-console-actions.test.js`, `integration/fr184-inventory-stocktake.test.js`, `unit/inventory-wip.test.js`, `unit/scm-console-routes.test.js`, `unit/warehouse-location.test.js` | ✅ |
| BR-029 | A bill of materials declares the loss it expects, and the buffer is issued up front — `ProductRecipe.scrapAllowanceFactor` is a fraction in `[0, 0.20]` (default 0, so every recipe predating it is unchanged), and the gross issue for a net requirement of Q is `ceil(Q × (1 + factor))`. What the buffer did not consume is reconciled at completion and stays where it is; what it did not cover is reported as a shortage that blocks the work order (`BLOCKED_SHORTAGE`) rather than a build that half-happened. Scrap is issued to the quarantine/scrap location so the loss is visible as a movement, never as a quantity that merely failed to arrive. | `app/(pm)/inventory/work-orders/page.jsx`, `app/api/inventory/customization-work-orders/[id]/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/customization-work-orders/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/kitting-work-orders/[id]/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/kitting-work-orders/route.js`, `modules/inventory/application/customization-work-order-service.js`, `modules/inventory/application/kitting-work-order-service.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/inventory-wip.js` | `integration/fr176-customization-work-order.test.js`, `integration/fr177-kitting-work-order.test.js`, `integration/fr182-scm-console-actions.test.js`, `unit/inventory-wip.test.js`, `unit/scm-console-routes.test.js` | ✅ |
| BR-030 | A unit that degrades in storage is guarded on issue, not merely reported — for a product declaring `maintenanceIntervalDays` and/or `maxStorageDays`, a lot's storage age runs from `lastMaintainedAt ?? manufacturedAt`. At the maintenance interval the lot is due and is surfaced as a task while remaining fully issuable; past `maxStorageDays` it is refused for issue and for kitting until a maintenance is recorded, which resets the clock. A product declaring neither is never affected. This is FEFO's second question — FR-155's FEFO decides which lot goes first, this decides which lots may go at all. | `app/api/inventory/shelf-life/route.js`, `modules/inventory/application/inventory-shelf-life-service.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/inventory-wip.js` | `integration/fr179-shelf-life-guard.test.js`, `integration/fr182-scm-console-actions.test.js`, `unit/inventory-wip.test.js`, `unit/scm-console-routes.test.js` | ✅ |
| BR-031 | What is already promised is subtracted before anything else is promised — `ATP = onHand − Σ committed reservations − Σ live quote reservations`, where a quote reservation is soft and expiring (7 days by default) and an order reservation is committed against a confirmed sales order. A reservation never writes the stock ledger, because a promise is not a physical fact and mixing the two would make on-hand disagree with a shelf; and a reservation is never deleted, only `RELEASED`, `CONVERTED` or `EXPIRED`, so a past refusal to promise stays explicable. Expiry is evaluated on read against the clock, so an ATP figure is correct whether or not a sweeper has run. | `app/(pm)/inventory/reservations/page.jsx`, `app/api/inventory/atp/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/reservations/[id]/route.js`, `app/api/inventory/reservations/route.js`, `modules/inventory/application/inventory-atp-service.js`, `modules/inventory/domain/inventory-wip.js` | `integration/fr180-atp-reservations.test.js`, `integration/fr181-smartgift-agent-tools.test.js`, `integration/fr182-scm-console-actions.test.js`, `unit/inventory-wip.test.js`, `unit/scm-console-routes.test.js` | ✅ |
| BR-032 | A tradeable finished set carries the FlowAccount normalized item code — `^[A-Z0-9]+-[0-9]+\([A-Z0-9_-]+\)$`: factory model, item count, package code (`TMS06-4(P-16)` is a four-item set in box P-16) — and a kitting work order is refused when its output has none, or one that does not match. The code is an **external** identifier and is stored as an attribute, never a key (BR-002): `Product.flowAccountSku`, unique per `(tenantId, flowAccountSku)`. It is not `Product.code`, whose FR-154 pattern deliberately has no room for parentheses, and not an `ExternalRef` row, because that table is unique installation-wide and two Tenants may legitimately sell a set the same factory model names. Components and packaging carry no such code at all, because they are never synchronised to FlowAccount. | `app/api/inventory/kitting-work-orders/route.js`, `modules/inventory/application/kitting-work-order-service.js` | `integration/fr177-kitting-work-order.test.js`, `integration/fr181-smartgift-agent-tools.test.js`, `integration/fr182-scm-console-actions.test.js`, `unit/inventory-costing.test.js`, `unit/scm-console-routes.test.js` | ✅ |
| BR-033 | A grant is withdrawn only by an explicit act that records who withdrew it and why. No referential action, cascade or other database side effect may change the scope of a grant, and none may widen one: a parent row whose deletion would reshape a grant must be refused until the grant is withdrawn. A status vocabulary that a resolver branches on must have a service that writes each of its values — a read filter over a column nothing writes is not an implemented control. | `app/api/platform/users/memberships/[id]/lifecycle/route.js`, `modules/identity/membership-grant-service.js`, `modules/identity/membership-lifecycle-service.js`, `modules/identity/resolve-viewer.js` | `integration/fr191-access-grant-lifecycle.test.js`, `integration/workspace-onboarding-flow.test.js`, `unit/fr061-per-business-domain-visibility.test.js`, `unit/fr076-product-owner-business-assignment.test.js`, `unit/membership-grant-service.test.js`, `unit/membership-lifecycle-service.test.js`, `unit/viewer-gate.test.js` | ✅ |
| BR-035 | Two people complete a cycle that touches money or stock; one person never does, however senior. A role that both records and confirms the same class of fact (recording a payment and verifying it; writing a purchase order and receiving against it) is a segregation-of-duties conflict, refused at assignment unless a Tenant owner overrides it with a recorded reason, and refused again at the transaction itself when the SAME person would record and confirm the SAME instance — a rule an OWNER does not bypass, because it is about needing a second person, not about holding a permission. An exemption for a genuinely one-person Business must be an explicit, audited attestation, never a silent one. | `modules/commerce/application/payment-service.js` | `integration/fr163-payment.test.js`, `integration/fr196-segregation-of-duties.test.js`, `integration/fr196-sod-cannot-be-assembled.test.js` | ✅ |
