| SDD-035 | An administration surface derives its read scope from the **same** field its write path authorizes on. `listUserPermissions` filters on `ownedBusinessIds`, which is exactly what `assertMembershipBusinessOwned` checks, so a row that would 404 on save cannot be listed as editable in the first place. Tenant-wide rows are the one deliberate exception — unmanageable here by design, yet shown because a hidden tenant-wide grant is worse than a read-only one — so the server, not the client, states `manageable` per row. | SDD-036 | Board and Schedule are **derived views over models other requirements already own** — WorkItem (FR-005), Project (FR-003), Milestone (FR-006). Neither introduces a persisted aggregate, an ordering column, or a second write path: a card opens the existing editor, and a bar is computed from dates the owning model already stores. Where such a view enumerates a vocabulary it does not own, it derives that vocabulary from `src/lib/validation/enums.js` — a hand-copied list is how a status ends up with nowhere to render. | `app/(pm)/projects/[projectId]/board/page.jsx`, `app/(pm)/projects/[projectId]/timeline/page.jsx`, `app/(pm)/timeline/page.jsx`, `modules/project-manager/application/project-service.js`, `modules/project-manager/views/universal/TimelineView.jsx` | `e2e/fr041-business-first.spec.js`, `e2e/smoke.spec.js`, `integration/fr072-project-service-authorization.test.js`, `integration/project-business-binding.test.js`, `integration/project-core.test.js`, `unit/fr063-board-columns.test.js`, `unit/project-list-contract.test.js`, `unit/project-work-route.test.js` | ✅ |
| SDD-037 | Import authorization is decided **once, on the resolved target Workspace**, in the pipeline that resolves it — not in each of the three route handlers, which is how three copies of a predicate drift. `authorizeImportTarget(viewer, workspace)` is the single entry point, it calls `ownsBusiness` rather than re-deriving ownership, and it is a required argument: a caller that omits the viewer throws at the call, in the manner `classify()` now requires its `scope`, so a fourth intake surface cannot be wired up unauthorized by simply not passing it. A Workspace above Business is refused rather than governed. The refusal is **evidence-backed, not conservatism**, and its justification is deliberately stated in two tenses because the first one expires. **Until FR-067 ships:** `Membership` is created in exactly two code paths and both set `businessId`, and the viewer contract exposes only Business-keyed grants (`visibleBusinessIds`, `ownedBusinessIds`, `domainsByBusinessId`) — so no principal can hold portfolio or tenant authority at all, and any rule written for it would be untestable, satisfiable by nothing and deniable of nothing. **Once FR-067 ships that premise is false and the refusal survives on a different, stronger reason:** a `WorkspaceMembership` is *collaboration* scope on the top-level container (keyed by `portfolioId`), and FR-067 AC-067.5 makes Tenant/Business/**Space**/Project assignment a separate audited mutation requiring the relevant owner authority — so holding it confers no write authority over a Space belonging to that Portfolio. AC-067.4 says the same thing from the read side: membership alone returns no hidden Project data, and import *creates* Project data. Two further facts make this more than a technicality — a portfolio-scoped Space carries **no `tenantId`** (`prisma/seed.js` WS-PLATFORM sets only `portfolioId`), so it sits above the BR-001 isolation boundary, and one Portfolio spans every Tenant beneath it. Opening a write path there on portfolio-level membership would put a write above the boundary the whole scope chain rests on. What would legitimately unlock it is neither FR-067 nor a looser predicate: it is a Space-level owner authority carried in the viewer contract — the FR-061 shape again — after which `AUTHORIZERS` gains an entry, never before. Inventing a rule at the end of a security fix is how the fix becomes the next incident. The three routes leave `docs/.route-viewer-baseline.json` when this lands; the baseline shrinks, it is never edited to accommodate them. | `app/api/import/commit/route.js`, `app/api/import/dry-run/route.js`, `app/api/import/xlsx/route.js`, `modules/project-manager/import/import-authorization.js`, `modules/project-manager/import/plan-import-service.js`, `modules/project-manager/import/plan-import-service.js` | `integration/external-ref-import.test.js`, `integration/import-target-authorization.test.js`, `integration/plan-import.test.js`, `integration/project-business-binding.test.js`, `unit/import-authorization.test.js` | ✅ |

| SDD-046 | A canvas gesture is an intake surface and gets no write path of its own: a drop builds an envelope delta and runs the BR-009 chain unchanged (validate → semantic check → dry run → single transaction → audit). The `preview` stage is **relocated, not skipped** — the optimistic edge already drawn in a visibly pending state *is* the preview, since a modal on every drop would make the canvas unusable while a silent commit would drop a stage the rule requires. A clean dry run commits; a failed one reverts the optimistic edge and prints the reason at the edge rather than in a toast that outlives its context. Undo rides the audit event. A future surface that cannot honour this adds a converter, never a second write path. | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |

| SDD-048 | Observability is one emitter and one correlation id, not logging scattered through the call path. `src/lib/observability` exposes a structured emitter whose sink is injectable (stdout by default, captured in tests) and whose field names are **allowlisted**: safety comes from what the emitter accepts, not from scrubbing what a caller passed, because a denylist only ever knows the secrets someone already thought of. An unknown field is omitted and its *name* listed in `unsafeFieldsOmitted`, so a dropped field is visible rather than silent. The correlation id is validated before use (`^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,64}$`) since it arrives as untrusted input and is echoed into records. | SDD-051 | Recording a reply is a **separate, narrow writer** in the crm module (`reply-record-service`), not a `direction: 'OUTBOUND'` call into `ingestLineMessage`. | SDD-057 | The seventeen-stage knowledge ingestion pipeline reuses the FR-071 execution ledger rather than declaring new models. At the storage layer that is free: `dataPipelineDefinitionId` and `pipelineStageId` are plain `String` columns with no enum, no relation and no check constraint (`prisma/schema.prisma`), so a new definition id and seventeen new `DPS-*` stage ids need no migration. At the contract layer it is not free, and saying so is the point of this decision: `zPipelineEvent` and `zPipelineRunInput` pin `dataPipelineDefinitionId` and `executionContractId` with `z.literal`, `pipelineStageId` is refined against the ten-entry FR-071 catalog (`src/platform/integrations/core/pipeline-tracking-contract.js`), and the tracking service materializes that one catalog on every run creation — so `DPL-KNOWLEDGE-INGEST-V1` is rejected today, and the first implementation slice must parameterize the FR-071 envelope by definition id before it can register anything. That is a change to implemented, tested FR-071 code, named here rather than discovered later. A second execution ledger remains the wrong answer: it would give one question two answers and make the reconciliation evidence something that has to be believed twice. `PipelineRecordEvent` already carries `docId` / `picId` / `factId` — written on every record event and read by provenance-filtered replay, with no producer supplying a non-null value today — and knowledge ingestion reuses their existing FR-071 meaning rather than redefining it. Current scope clarification (ADR-073, 2026-09-08): the contract/catalog parameterization is implemented. The prohibition is on a second execution ledger; the approved isolated slice adds seven lineage/batch/evidence/cursor/receipt models without replacing FR-071. | SDD-058 | Knowledge ingestion stage execution location is policy-driven, not deployment-driven. The specification's split (Stages 1–8 local edge, 9–14 cloud GKS, 15–17 selected execution) is an illustrative default, not the contract: the binding input is the data's own classification, so RESTRICTED knowledge carrying `cloud_processing_allowed = false` runs all seventeen stages locally and no topology or environment change may override that. Location is therefore resolved per object from FR-111's policy fields at each stage boundary, never read from deployment configuration — a data boundary that configuration can widen is not a boundary. | `modules/knowledge/classification.js` | `unit/knowledge-classification.test.js` | ✅ |
| SDD-059 | Chunking is a pure calculator in the knowledge lane, with no persistence and no model. `src/modules/knowledge/chunking.js` takes parsed blocks plus a scope and a provenance and returns chunk objects; it opens no database, writes no table and declares no Prisma model, so the knowledge charter's `owns_models: []` stays true (SDD-057, ADR-050). Persisting chunks is a later slice and will need a charter change — pinning the boundary here makes it a decision rather than an oversight. Token counting is a whitespace approximation on purpose: a model tokenizer would couple Stage 7 to whichever embedding model Stage 15 happens to use, and this number exists to choose a split point, not to be billed against. Exactness belongs at the embedding boundary, where the model is known. Current scope clarification (ADR-073, 2026-09-08): chunking.js remains pure. The approved separate GenesisRAG17 adapter now persists KnowledgeRawArtifact, KnowledgeParsedArtifact and KnowledgeChunk through repository interfaces; the charter lists its seven models. | SDD-060 | Entity recognition is a seam with a deterministic default, not a model dependency. `extractEntityCandidates` takes an optional `recognizer`; the default recognises legal-form organisation patterns only — `บริษัท … จำกัด`, `ห้างหุ้นส่วนจำกัด …`, `… Co., Ltd.` — because in those the suffix IS the evidence: the grammar announces the type without anyone recognising the name inside it. Person, product, location and the rest of §13's type list need a model, and a model in the knowledge lane would couple Stage 8 to whatever recognises Thai and English text this month (the SDD-059 reasoning, one stage over). An empty seam was rejected as the alternative: shipping a hook with nothing behind it is what makes Stage 15 embedding read as delivered when it is not, and this requirement declines to repeat that shape. `normalized_name` is lexical and uses only the mention itself — trim, collapse, strip the legal wrapper. | SDD-061 | A normalizer that cannot decide reports the ambiguity instead of resolving it — and reports it by withholding the canonical value, not by attaching a warning beside one. A warning next to a readable field is a warning somebody skips: if `canonical` holds a guess, a caller will use it. So an undecidable value returns `canonical: null` with the reason, and the caller has nothing else to read. The case that sets the rule is a Thai date like `25/8/26`, which is 2526 BE or 2026 CE with nothing in the string to separate them — forty-three years apart, both plausible, and no downstream check can catch the wrong one. The same withholding covers an undeclared field order (`5/8/69`, where day and month are both twelve or under) and a date that does not exist (`31/2/2569`, which must never roll into March). Six of §9's twelve categories are normalized; currency, unit, product code, country/region, timezone and identifier format are declined for a stronger reason than missing locale data — their canonical form is business-configured, so one invented here would be wrong in a way that looks authoritative. | `modules/knowledge/normalization.js`, `modules/line-oa-studio/ui/index.js`, `modules/line-oa-studio/ui/LineStudioDashboard.jsx`, `modules/line-oa-studio/ui/LineStudioDesignHub.jsx`, `modules/line-oa-studio/ui/LineStudioFlexEditor.jsx`, `modules/line-oa-studio/ui/LineStudioFlowDesigner.jsx`, `modules/line-oa-studio/ui/LineStudioLiffApp.jsx`, `modules/line-oa-studio/ui/LineStudioProjects.jsx`, `modules/line-oa-studio/ui/LineStudioSettings.jsx`, `modules/line-oa-studio/ui/LineStudioShell.jsx`, `modules/line-oa-studio/ui/LineStudioTeam.jsx`, `modules/line-oa-studio/ui/LineStudioTemplates.jsx`, `modules/line-oa-studio/ui/mockStudioData.js` | `integration/fr109-knowledge-ingestion-executor.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-normalization.test.js`, `unit/line-oa-settings-consolidation.test.js`, `unit/line-studio-dashboard-render.test.js` | ✅ |
| SDD-062 | Widening what knowledge may carry must not widen what knowledge is served. `business-contract.js` holds two decisions that share a word: the record schema, which says what may be STORED, and the query filter, which says what is SERVED to a public surface. FR-111 widens the first to the four-level lattice and leaves the second at `PUBLIC`, and the two are separate on purpose rather than by oversight. The failure this pins against is not somebody deliberately opening the filter — it is somebody reading `record.sensitivity === 'PUBLIC'`, seeing that the enum now has four values, and correcting the comparison because it looks stale. It is not stale; it is the boundary. The packet stops asserting its own label and verifies it instead: it refuses to wrap a record above `PUBLIC` rather than stamping `PUBLIC` on whatever it was handed, so a future widening of the filter fails loudly instead of leaking. The lattice lives in `src/lib/validation/enums.js` and is named `KNOWLEDGE_SENSITIVITY_LEVELS` because `sensitivity` already means FR-026 action sensitivity (LOW/HIGH) elsewhere; `write-tools.js` rejects an action sensitivity outside LOW/HIGH so a knowledge value cannot reach that path, but `agent/auth-context.js` validates nothing and already defaults to the string `PUBLIC` — so the separation rests on naming there rather than on a guard, and saying so is the point of pinning it. | `modules/knowledge/classification.js` | `unit/knowledge-classification.test.js` | ✅ |
| SDD-063 | The parser produces the block shape Stage 7 already consumes. `parseDocument` returns `structure` as an array of `{ type: heading or text, level, text }`, which is exactly the `blocks` argument `chunkDocument` reads — so Stage 2 hands Stage 7 its input rather than the two agreeing by coincidence across two test suites that each build the shape. **Stage 7 owns the shape**; it existed first and its tests define it, and the parser conforms to it. The seam is held by a test that feeds real parser output straight into `chunkDocument` and asserts on the resulting chunks, because a written statement that two shapes match is prose, and prose is not a contract — the same reason a green governance run does not prove a link resolves. Markdown and plain text are parsed here; PDF, DOCX, OCR and vision layout are not, and are received instead through FR-071's `smartgift.document-intake.v1` front door, which validates someone else's extraction rather than performing one. | `modules/knowledge/genesisrag17-source.js`, `modules/knowledge/parsing.js` | `unit/genesisrag17-parser-2.test.js`, `unit/genesisrag17-source.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-ingestion-job.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-parsing.test.js` | ✅ |
| SDD-064 | Declaring knowledge derived is a claim that names its sources, not an exemption from having any. Spec §8 forbids publishing a Fact or Relation whose source cannot be traced "unless explicitly declared DERIVED / INFERRED / COMPUTED", which read naively is an escape hatch: type the word and the requirement lifts. The declaration therefore exempts an object from having a RAW source and never from having sources — an object that declares itself derived and can name nothing is not derived, it is unattributed, and it does not publish. Naming is not enough either: `assertPublishable` requires a resolver and refuses a named source that nothing resolves, because a check that accepts an unresolvable name lets the declaration back itself, which is the laundering this decision exists to stop arriving through the door the decision built. There is deliberately no shape-only mode; whether a named source exists is the question, and a permissive default would answer a different one. | `modules/knowledge/provenance.js` | `unit/knowledge-provenance.test.js` | ✅ |
| SDD-065 | Cross-tenant deduplication is made inexpressible rather than forbidden. The tenant is folded into the ingestion identity hash itself, so two tenants holding a byte-identical document cannot produce the same key and there is no comparison to prohibit — a rule enforced by what can be constructed outlives one enforced by a check that has to fire, and a check can be deleted by someone who reads it as redundant. Section 11 lists five dedup strategies and this implements three: exact checksum, canonical checksum and source-native id, all of which read what the artifacts say about themselves. Content similarity and structural similarity are declined, and not merely as unimplemented: both require a threshold, and a threshold chosen in this module would be a judgement about which documents count as "the same" made by whoever typed the number and inherited by everyone after. The same reasoning declines inferring a lineage from a filename — `contract-v1.pdf` beside `contract-v2.pdf` is a hint a human left, not a claim the system can verify. | — | `integration/fr109-ingestion-run-identity.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-ingestion-job.test.js` | 🔴 no anchor |
| SDD-066 | A pipeline definition is a registry entry, and a stage id is validated against its own definition's catalog rather than against the union of every catalog. FR-071 pinned `dataPipelineDefinitionId` and `executionContractId` with `z.literal` and refined `pipelineStageId` against one ten-entry list; SDD-057 named the parameterization this slice inherits, and this decision fixes its shape. `PIPELINE_DEFINITIONS` maps a definition id to its execution contract id and its own frozen stage catalog, the envelope validates the PAIR so a run may not claim `DPL-KNOWLEDGE-INGEST-V1` under `EXC-DATA-MIGRATION-V1`, and `createPipelineRun` materializes the catalog belonging to the run's own definition instead of the one constant it happened to import. Validating a stage against the union was rejected as the easier change: it would let a Supabase migration run report a `DPS-KI-EMBED` step and pass every check, which is the ADR-050 D3 tier boundary being crossed inside a validator that reported no problem. The ten `DPS-*` ids keep their catalog and their meaning; the seventeen `DPS-KI-*` ids get their own. | `app/api/pipelines/runs/route.js`, `modules/knowledge/ingestion-job.js`, `platform/integrations/core/knowledge-ingestion-executor.js`, `platform/integrations/core/pipeline-tracking-contract.js` | `integration/fr109-ingestion-run-identity.test.js`, `integration/fr109-knowledge-ingestion-executor.test.js`, `integration/fr110-knowledge-reporter-receiver.test.js`, `integration/fr119-knowledge-ingestion-quarantine.test.js`, `integration/openapi-docs.test.js`, `unit/asset-management-pipeline-contract.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-ingestion-job.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-published-snapshot-contract.test.js`, `unit/pipeline-tracking-route.test.js`, `unit/platform/fr129-catalog-publication-gate.test.js`, `unit/platform/knowledge-ingestion-catalog.test.js`, `unit/platform/pipeline-tracking-contract.test.js`, `unit/platform/pipeline-tracking-service.test.js` | ✅ |
| SDD-067 | A knowledge ingestion run input is derived whole from the artifact's ingestion identity, its correlation id included, because FR-071 compares more than the key. `createPipelineRun` returns `UNCHANGED` for a repeated `idempotencyKey` only when `requestHash` also matches, and that hash covers the entire parsed input — so a second ingestion of the same artifact carrying a fresh correlation id raises 409 `idempotency key was reused with different input` rather than being the no-op BR-021 requires. `knowledgeIngestionRunInput` therefore computes every field from the artifact, and re-ingestion is byte-identical by construction rather than by the caller remembering to make it so. The cost is stated rather than hidden: the second attempt's own correlation is not recorded, because there is one run and it was correlated once. Relaxing FR-071's comparison to the key alone was rejected — that comparison is what catches a key reused for genuinely different input, and weakening it for one definition weakens it for the definition already relying on it. | `modules/knowledge/ingestion-job.js` | `integration/fr109-ingestion-run-identity.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-ingestion-job.test.js` | ✅ |
| SDD-068 | Field-name mapping between stage functions is made explicit and singular, never left for each caller to reconstruct. FR-116 names an artifact's content hash `checksum` (spec §8); FR-117 names the identical value `content_hash` (spec §29, BR-021). Two accurate module-local names for one fact is correct inside each module and a hazard at their boundary — a caller supplying two copies keeps them in sync by discipline, which is the exact shape BR-021's own field-separator defect took (a hand-written comparison of two keys, made without reading the one compared against). `runKnowledgeIngestionStages` takes `content_hash` once, from the artifact, and renames it to `checksum` in exactly the one call that needs the other name. The same discipline governs a chunk's `scope`: FR-111 classifies against `{ scope: { tenantId, businessId }, sensitivity, ... }`, but a chunk's `scope` field carries the identity pair alone — the shape the FR-111-into-FR-112 seam test already fixed — never the whole classification object, which would nest a field named `scope` inside a field named `scope`. | `modules/knowledge/stage-runner.js` | `unit/knowledge-stage-runner-trace.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-stage-runner.test.js` | ✅ |
| SDD-069 | The ledger-writing wiring binds one artifact identity to one run to one document's worth of evidence, and every event's idempotency key is derived from that identity alone — never from the caller's `documentId`. `ingestKnowledgeDocument` writes a `RECORD_STARTED` / `RECORD_SUCCEEDED` pair (`docId` bound to `documentId`) around a `STEP_STARTED` / `STEP_SUCCEEDED` pair per Tier 1 stage, using the `PipelineStep` rows `createPipelineRun` already materializes from `KNOWLEDGE_INGESTION_STAGE_CATALOG` — no second step is created. The run is never marked finished: nine of the seventeen catalog steps belong to GKS and GenesisBlockDB (ADR-050 D3) and this repository does not execute them, so sending `RUN_FINISHED` here would claim a run seven-seventeenths done is complete. A stage failure inside FR-118's composition is not caught, classified or written as `STEP_FAILED` — the composition is one synchronous call with no partial result, so nothing at this layer can name which of the seven stages threw without restructuring FR-118's contract, which this slice does not do; the already-registered run stays at `QUEUED` with every step `NOT_STARTED`; an honest "attempted, nothing recorded" rather than a guess. The whole function is resumable by construction: replaying it with the same artifact replays every event's own idempotency check (FR-071) rather than needing a resume branch of its own. | `platform/integrations/core/knowledge-ingestion-executor.js` | `integration/fr109-knowledge-ingestion-executor.test.js`, `integration/fr110-knowledge-reporter-receiver.test.js`, `integration/fr119-knowledge-ingestion-quarantine.test.js` | ✅ |
| SDD-070 | NFR-020's per-stage counts are written by reusing `PipelineStep`'s own `actualCount`/`insertedCount`/`failedCount` columns — present since FR-071, unwritten by anything until this decision. `zPipelineEvent` gains three optional fields under the same names; `stageData()` persists them only when a caller actually supplies one, so a `STEP_HEARTBEAT` or a plain success from the Supabase migration path — every existing caller — leaves them exactly as they were, and an already-recorded count is never overwritten with an omitted one. `actualCount` is `records_in`, `insertedCount` is `records_out`, `failedCount` is `records_failed` — a field-name mapping decision, the same shape as SDD-068's `checksum`/`content_hash`. `records_quarantined` and `retry_count` are not wired to anything: BR-022's quarantine vocabulary and replay-within-run don't exist for knowledge ingestion, and a stored 0 for either would be indistinguishable from a measured zero — the exact defect found on `PipelineRun` while building this. This fix does not reach backward: every migration-pipeline run and every knowledge-ingestion run that predates this slice keeps its `@default(0)`, and `actualCount`/`failedCount` are `Int` NOT NULL with nothing correlating "never measured" — no signal distinguishes an old unmeasured zero from a new measured one. The monitor's `fmt()` already renders `null` as `—` rather than `0`; it has simply never been given a null. Closing that gap needs a migration of its own and is not this slice's to make. | `platform/integrations/core/knowledge-ingestion-executor.js`, `platform/integrations/core/pipeline-tracking-contract.js` | `integration/fr071-pipeline-step-count-aggregation.test.js`, `integration/fr109-knowledge-ingestion-executor.test.js`, `integration/fr110-knowledge-reporter-receiver.test.js`, `integration/fr119-knowledge-ingestion-quarantine.test.js`, `unit/asset-management-pipeline-contract.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-published-snapshot-contract.test.js`, `unit/platform/fr129-catalog-publication-gate.test.js`, `unit/platform/knowledge-ingestion-catalog.test.js`, `unit/platform/pipeline-tracking-contract.test.js`, `unit/platform/pipeline-tracking-service.test.js` | ✅ |
| SDD-071 | The execution monitor has read `PipelineRun.actualCount` / `.failedCount` since before anything wrote them — `@default(0)`, and `mode-bodies.jsx` renders `Failed` in the normal ink specifically because it is zero. That is not an empty panel; it is a false one, indistinguishable on screen from a real zero. `recordPipelineEvent` now aggregates the run's counts from every `PipelineStep` row sharing its `runId` whenever an event supplies a count — real summed integers, not nulls, so the same `\|\| 0` coercion in `runSummary`/`stepSummary` that would have silently absorbed a null has nothing to undermine. Aggregate-on-completion, not finalization-only: a run half done showing a final-looking zero would be the same defect in slower motion, and knowledge-ingestion runs never reach `RUN_FINISHED` in this repository (ADR-050 D3) — finalization-only would mean never. The aggregate sums every step row sharing a `runId`, which assumes one row per stage per run; `(runId, pipelineStageId)` carries no uniqueness constraint, only a non-unique index, so a stage retried under a fresh `executionStepId` would be double-counted rather than reporting its true count. Nothing today constructs that — SDD-069 materializes each stage's step once and reuses it; replay creates its steps under a new `runId`, never summed into the original. A second row is not a hypothetical the design left open: the step state machine retries a stage in place — `REPLAYING: ['REPLAYING', 'RUNNING', 'SUCCEEDED', 'FAILED']`, on the same row — and ADR-030's own rejected-alternatives table names the other direction, reusing ids across a retry, as overwriting history and making the result non-auditable. So `(runId, pipelineStageId)` staying unenforced is a real gap against the design's own intent, not an open question — `@@unique([runId, pipelineStageId])` is very likely correct, pending a check for existing duplicate rows before any migration can apply it. Pinned by two tests, not fixed ahead of that check: a fake-db unit test recording today's behaviour against a fake that cannot enforce a uniqueness constraint and would silently go stale the moment a migration lands, and a real-database integration test constructing the same duplicate directly — the one that will actually start failing, at the `create` call rather than at an assertion, the day the constraint exists. | — | `integration/fr071-pipeline-step-count-aggregation.test.js`, `integration/fr119-knowledge-ingestion-quarantine.test.js`, `integration/fr129-catalog-publication-gate.test.js`, `unit/platform/fr129-catalog-publication-gate.test.js` | 🔴 no anchor |
| SDD-072 | FR-118's seven stage-invocation steps are extracted into one shared definition (`TIER1_STEPS`) that both `runKnowledgeIngestionStages` and a new `runKnowledgeIngestionStagesWithTrace` iterate, so the field mappings — checksum from content_hash, a chunk's scope as the identity pair alone, and the rest SDD-068 already named — are written once rather than duplicated between a throwing caller and a trace-collecting one. `runKnowledgeIngestionStages` keeps its exact throw-on-first-failure contract; all fifteen of its existing tests pass unchanged, because its observable behaviour did not change, only its internals. The new function never throws itself: a stage's exception becomes data (`{success, stages, envelope, partialEnvelope, failedStage, error}`), because the reason to call it instead of the throwing version is to keep going long enough to report where it stopped. `errorRef` stays redacted on the FR-071 ledger, per that contract's own repeated description of `PipelineRecordEvent`/`PipelineStep` as carrying "redacted" evidence, most concretely AC-071.28 ("agents emit only append-only, redacted events"). This was a **caller-side convention with no contract enforcement** at the moment this slice was written — `errorRef: z.string().max(1000)` accepted anything. A peer session found the same gap independently while reviewing this slice and closed it separately: `errorRef` is now a validated reference token (SDD-073, `/^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._:/#@-]*$/`, max 200). Both `errorRef` values this slice writes — `ki-quarantine://{identity}/{stage}` and `ki-quarantine://{identity}/record` — were checked against that pattern directly before this row was written and conform with room to spare. `error_message` (BR-022's field) therefore lives only in the quarantine envelope this executor returns to its own caller; the ledger's `errorRef` holds a stable reference (`ki-quarantine://{identity}/{stage}`) instead. `retryable`, a boolean, cannot carry BR-022's three-way classification — only `RETRYABLE` maps to `true`; `REVIEW_REQUIRED` maps to `false` alongside `NON_RETRYABLE`, since "a human must act" and "retrying alone will not" are both true of it, and the full classification travels in the envelope regardless of what the shared boolean can hold. `retry_count` is always `0` and `first_failed_at`/`last_failed_at` always coincide — no retry-within-run mechanism exists (the same gap SDD-071 names for NFR-020's identical field), so there is exactly one attempt to time. | `modules/knowledge/quarantine.js`, `modules/knowledge/stage-runner.js`, `platform/integrations/core/knowledge-ingestion-executor.js` | `integration/fr109-knowledge-ingestion-executor.test.js`, `integration/fr110-knowledge-reporter-receiver.test.js`, `integration/fr119-knowledge-ingestion-quarantine.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-quarantine.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-stage-runner-trace.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-stage-runner.test.js` | ✅ |
| SDD-073 | `errorRef` on the pipeline event envelope is a reference to an error, never the error itself, and the contract now enforces the shape rather than trusting the caller. FR-071 states the redaction rule four times — `PipelineStep`/`PipelineRecordEvent` as "one redacted record outcome per attempt", the "redacted append-only evidence outbox", keeping "restricted document payloads separate from redacted pipeline events", and AC-071.28's "emit only append-only, redacted" events — and enforced it nowhere: the field was `z.string().trim().min(1).max(1000)`, which accepts a stack trace, an exception message or a quoted document fragment, on a ledger that is append-only and therefore cannot take a payload back. The rule lived entirely in caller discipline at a moment when BR-022 was about to make the field carry real failure text for the first time. `errorRef` is now a reference token — ASCII alphanumerics plus the separators a handle uses, capped at 200 — which every value in the repository already satisfied (`err://event-1`). The rule is an ALLOWED ALPHABET rather than a rejected separator on purpose: rejecting whitespace reads as sufficient in English and is not, because Thai does not separate words with spaces, so a whole Thai sentence is one whitespace-free token and would have passed a space/newline check untouched — in a product whose user-facing copy is Thai, that is the likelier shape of a real error message, not the exotic one. Stated as a bound rather than a guarantee: this blocks the accident — passing `err.message` or `err.stack` straight through — and not a determined caller, who can still join a sentence with underscores; a conforming value is not evidence that the reference carries nothing sensitive. The full human-readable message belongs in the caller's own return value, never on the shared ledger. | `platform/integrations/core/pipeline-tracking-contract.js` | `unit/asset-management-pipeline-contract.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-published-snapshot-contract.test.js`, `unit/platform/fr129-catalog-publication-gate.test.js`, `unit/platform/knowledge-ingestion-catalog.test.js`, `unit/platform/pipeline-tracking-contract.test.js` | ✅ |
| SDD-074 | Plugin auth is one canonical `/api/plugin/auth` route family over three identity-owned models (`PluginInstallation`, `PluginAuthorizationCode`, `PluginSession`), with every handler thin over `src/modules/identity/plugin-auth-service.js`. `/authorize` carries two operations for one reason: a GET that renders and never acts (it 302s to `/plugin/authorize` without reading a session or touching the database) and a POST that acts and only from that screen's own form. The consent screen's facts are server-derived through the same service functions the mint uses — `assertPluginAuthorizeParameters` for the parameters and `derivePluginCapabilitiesForViewer` for the capability list — so the screen cannot validate one request while the mint performs another. The two consent tokens are HMACs over `ZURI_SESSION_SECRET` and are stored nowhere, so the gate needs no model and no DDL. Single-use is a **database predicate, not a read-then-write**: consumption is `updateMany({ consumedAt: null })` inside the same transaction that creates the session, so two concurrent redemptions produce one count of 1 and one count of 0 and the loser throws before anything is written. The PKCE challenge is compared with `timingSafeEqual`, never `===`, because it is a secret comparison. Client configuration is read from the environment, exact and fail-closed. The plugin session is neither the browser session nor the MCP continuation id, and plugin input never authors Tenant/Business/Membership/role scope — `resolveViewer` is the only scope resolver, called without `platformGrant`. `POST /token` and `POST /revoke` are structurally exempt from the Check-10 route-viewer ratchet, the same class as the `/api/auth` lifecycle endpoints: their only credential is the one-time code or the opaque bearer, so requiring a browser viewer there would be the broken boundary, not the safe one. | `app/(entry)/plugin/authorize/page.jsx`, `app/api/plugin/auth/authorize/route.js`, `app/api/plugin/auth/capabilities/route.js`, `app/api/plugin/auth/revoke/route.js`, `app/api/plugin/auth/token/route.js`, `modules/identity/plugin-auth-service.js`, `modules/identity/plugin-consent-access.js`, `modules/identity/plugin-consent-view.js`, `modules/identity/plugin-consent.js` | `e2e/fr123-plugin-consent.spec.js`, `integration/fr123-plugin-auth-reaper.test.js`, `integration/fr123-plugin-consent-gate.test.js`, `unit/fr123-plugin-auth-route.test.js`, `unit/fr123-plugin-auth-service.test.js`, `unit/fr123-plugin-consent-view.test.js`, `unit/fr123-plugin-consent.test.js` | ✅ |
| SDD-075 | An approval gate's evidence goes in `PipelineGateDecision.evidenceJson`, not in typed columns on the shared ledger. The column has existed since FR-071 in both `prisma/schema.prisma` and `supabase/migrations/20260820221703_smartgift_pipeline_tracking.sql` (`text NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'`, under FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY with the other five tables) and nothing has ever written it: `zGate` is a five-field `.strict()` object with no evidence member, `recordPipelineEvent` passes the literal `'{}'`, and `gateSummary` drops the field from the read model — so the evidence column is present, tested for existence, and unreachable. That is the same "column present since FR-071, unwritten by anything" shape SDD-070 found on `PipelineStep`, and it takes the same fix: one optional member on the event envelope, persisted only when a caller actually supplies it, so every existing decision row and every existing caller is unchanged. Adding `catalogVersion`, `vaultId`, `approvedAt` and `approvedBy` columns as CR-003 proposes was rejected on the ledger's own terms. SDD-066 made these six tables definition-neutral precisely so a second pipeline definition could share them; `catalogVersion` is one definition's vocabulary, and putting it in a table both definitions write is the union-catalog mistake SDD-066 already refused, relocated from the validator into the schema where no validator can refuse it. `approvedAt` and `approvedBy` are additionally redundant — the `APPROVED` row's own `createdAt` and `decidedByPersonId` are those two facts, and a second copy of a fact is a second answer to it. `vaultId` is resolved per API-010 from scope (FR-057, ADR-022) and is not the ledger's to hold. The cost is named rather than hidden: `evidenceJson` is untyped `text`, so gate evidence is exactly as checkable as the Zod member that writes it and no more — which is why that member is a declared part of this decision instead of left for the first caller to invent, and why a reader must not infer a schema from a sample row. | `platform/integrations/core/pipeline-gate-compliance.js`, `platform/integrations/core/pipeline-tracking-contract.js`, `platform/integrations/core/pipeline-tracking-service.js` | `integration/fr129-catalog-publication-gate.test.js`, `unit/asset-management-pipeline-contract.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-published-snapshot-contract.test.js`, `unit/platform/fr129-catalog-publication-gate.test.js`, `unit/platform/knowledge-ingestion-catalog.test.js`, `unit/platform/pipeline-tracking-contract.test.js`, `unit/platform/pipeline-tracking-service.test.js` | ✅ |
| SDD-076 | A bound repository's **metadata converges on FR-081; its file contents are never persisted.** FR-081(c) makes verbatim persistence the rule for every acquisition channel — raw payloads are replayable evidence, translated by a separate path — and a repository content projection is the first channel where obeying that rule is the defect rather than the discipline. A push delivery's metadata is exactly what `RawExternalRecord` is for and goes there unchanged: head SHA, changed paths, actor, timestamps, under the one envelope with `sourceType: 'WEBHOOK'`. A blob's bytes do not, and the reason is a property of the source rather than a preference about storage: a bound repository is an arbitrary external tree whose contents this repository cannot inspect in advance. CR-006 supplies the concrete case — the repository CR-004 proposes to surface held three spreadsheets of real customer data, tracked in git on a public remote, found by byte-comparing a tree rather than by any check that runs here. Persisting that content would put personal data in a table the erasure path has no route to: FR-022 erases through `Person`/`Customer`, SEC-005 consent is attested on the FR-023 `Customer` row per Business (SDD-053), and rows inside a spreadsheet inside a blob have neither — so the record would be personal data that is unconsented and, by construction, unerasable. The projection therefore reads through and renders; it stores no file content, and both the render and the metadata lane are bounded by a declared path scope, deny-by-default, in the shape SEC-009 already applies to LINE knowledge access. `ProjectRepository.pathScope` is the field that carries such a scope today and is already threaded end-to-end — input schema, service, read model, UI pill, plan and xlsx import — but nothing treats it as a boundary, so it is descriptive metadata that this decision would make load-bearing, which is a change of meaning to state rather than to assume. Named rather than glossed: changed *paths* are metadata, but a path can itself be personal data — CR-006 declines to reproduce the filenames it found for precisely that reason — so the metadata lane is not clean by virtue of being metadata and is bounded by the same scope. **The rejected alternative is the literal reading of FR-081(c)**, and it is rejected explicitly because it is what a reader who finds that clause and not this one will do; a carve-out from a requirement's own default is invisible unless it is written where the default is written. Also rejected, on the ledger's own terms: CR-004's `githubRepoUrl`/`githubBranch`/`githubSyncStatus`/`lastCommitSha`/`lastGithubSyncAt` as columns on `Business`. `Repository` has carried the first two since FR-008 and has been Business-owned since FR-073; the last three are `SyncCursor.cursorValue`, `SyncCursor.lastSuccessAt` and `IngestionRun.status`, which is what a watermark, a success time and a run outcome already mean. Columns on `Business` would additionally cap a Business at one repository, and put a foreign URL and commit SHA on a scope entity — the case BR-002 names with "GitHub id" among its examples. | — | `unit/integrations-page-claims.test.js` | 🔴 no anchor |
| SDD-077 | A second `knowledge_type` in `business_knowledge` needs its own registered query and its own contract version, because every existing query filters on no type at all. `registeredPredicate` (`src/modules/knowledge/postgres-business-knowledge.js`) builds three predicates — `product_detail`, `product_compare`, and `product_search`'s `ilike` across `product_code`, `name`, `category` and `description` — and the SQL around them constrains `tenant_id`, `business_id`, `sensitivity = 'PUBLIC'` and `is_active`, and nothing else. Today that is safe by accident rather than by design: `knowledge_type` is check-constrained to `'PRODUCT'` in the DDL and `z.literal('PRODUCT')` in `business-contract.js`, and one kind cannot be mistaken for another while there is only one kind. The moment FR-131 widens that constraint, those same three queries answer product questions with rate rows and `product_compare` on two rate codes returns them as products — a wrong answer delivered on the LINE surface with nothing anywhere reporting a problem. So the widening is one decision in four places, not one: the DDL check, the Zod literal, a `contract_version` that is no longer `'1.0.0'`, and a fourth registered query that names the type it serves. Holding rates in a new `ShippingRateMatrix` Prisma model instead, as CR-005 proposes, was rejected on this repository's own terms rather than on taste: it puts one Business's published prices in two stores with two provenance stories — one with `as_of`, `approved_at`, `source_sha256` and an FR-129 signature, one with `updatedAt` — and the second cannot say what a past quotation was priced from. This is deliberately **not** SDD-062 and points the other way. That decision is about `sensitivity`, and says widening what knowledge may CARRY must not widen what is SERVED. Widening `knowledge_type` must widen what is served, because the agent has to be able to quote a rate, so the containment here is a typed query rather than an unchanged filter — and reading SDD-062 as already covering this case is the specific mistake this decision exists to stop. | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |

| SDD-090 | The located ledger and the two WIP work orders are additive columns plus four tables, never a second stock system: `WarehouseLocation` and `StockReservation` are new Business-scoped rows; `CustomizationWorkOrder` and `KittingWorkOrder` are new work-order rows that hold *intent and progress* while the FR-155 ledger keeps holding *fact*, so a work order can be queried for "how many are on the laser bench" without any quantity being stored twice. Every addition to an existing table is nullable or defaulted (`StockMovement.sourceLocationId`, `targetLocationId`, `costSatang`, `customerId`, `salesOrderId`, `workOrderId`; `Product.itemKind`, `dedicatedCustomerId`, `dedicatedSalesOrderId`, `maintenanceIntervalDays`, `maxStorageDays`, `flowAccountSku`; `ProductLot.lastMaintainedAt`; `ProductRecipe.scrapAllowanceFactor`), so every row and every reader written before this change stays correct. Work orders write the ledger only through Inventory's own `appendMovement`, which is what keeps append-only, FEFO, insufficient-stock refusal and one audit row per movement true for WIP as well. | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
| SDD-091 | An agent tool is a thin adapter over an application service and adds no authority of its own: each SmartGift tool takes the caller's already-resolved viewer, calls the same exported service a page or route would call, and returns its result — no direct Prisma query, no scope assembled from tool arguments, no Business id the caller did not already prove. The split across the two existing registries is by effect, not by convenience: read tools land in the Gate E `createToolRegistry` (which refuses anything not `readOnly: true`) and write actions in the Gate F `createWriteToolRegistry`, where the two irreversible dispatches declare `HIGH` sensitivity so the action gate demands step-up. Tool JSON schemas are generated from the same Zod contracts the services parse, so a tool cannot advertise a shape the service would reject. | `modules/agent/tools/smartgift-inventory-tools.js` | `integration/fr181-smartgift-agent-tools.test.js` | ✅ |
| SDD-092 | Authorization invariants are held by the database, not by application convention: scope shape and status vocabulary by CHECK, one live grant per person per scope by partial unique indexes excluding REVOKED, Tenant ancestry by composite foreign keys against `Business (id, tenantId)`, and parent deletion by RESTRICT. Provenance columns live on the grant row rather than only in the append-only stream, because an access review queries current state and because two of the three historical writers audited under other entity types, leaving half of the live grants with no MEMBERSHIP event naming their author. | `modules/identity/membership-grant-service.js`, `modules/identity/membership-lifecycle-service.js` | `unit/membership-grant-service.test.js`, `unit/membership-lifecycle-service.test.js` | ✅ |
| SDD-094 | One generalised invitation table, not three parallel ones: `AccessInvite` (renamed from `WorkspaceInvite`) carries `scopeType` (PORTFOLIO / TENANT / BUSINESS) with a CHECK binding it to exactly one of `portfolioId`/`tenantId`/`businessId`, and an addressee CHECK requiring at least one of `targetPersonId`/`invitedEmail`/`invitedLineUserId`. `RoleBinding.businessId` becomes nullable to admit the TENANT scope ADR-077 D3 declared and left unresolved; `resolveViewer` expands a TENANT binding to every ACTIVE Business the Tenant holds at each request — including Businesses created after the grant, which a TENANT binding reaches without a new assignment, independent of `visibleBusinessIds`, and `hasPermission` is unchanged. `PlatformGrant` drops its unconditional `(personId, capability)` unique in favour of one scoped to `status = 'ACTIVE'`, so a superseded or expired grant survives as history rather than blocking a renewal. | `modules/identity/access-invite-service.js` | `integration/fr195-access-invite.test.js`, `integration/fr196-segregation-of-duties.test.js` | ✅ |

| SEC-021 | Knowledge ingestion never deduplicates across tenants and never embeds credentials or access tokens: deduplication compares only within one tenant's scope, because two tenants holding a content-identical document hold two facts, and collapsing them is a cross-tenant disclosure wearing the costume of an optimisation (spec §34; SEC-001). Secrets are redacted before parsing and are never embedded, never indexed, and never written into a log line or a quarantine record — a quarantine store is exactly where an unredacted failure payload would come to rest. Source authenticity is verified wherever the channel permits it; transport and sensitive storage are encrypted. | `modules/knowledge/dedup.js`, `modules/knowledge/ingestion-job.js`, `modules/knowledge/structured-record-policy.js` | `integration/fr109-ingestion-run-identity.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-dedup.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-ingestion-job.test.js`, `unit/knowledge-zero-pii-policy.test.js` | ✅ |
| SEC-022 | Plugin auth is a separate public-client boundary: no password or browser cookie enters the plugin; authorization codes are PKCE S256-bound, single-use and stored only as hashes; access tokens are opaque, 15-minute and hash-stored; a replayed code is refused **and** revokes the session it already minted (RFC 9700 §4.1.1); revocation is idempotent and answers identically whether or not the token existed; client id, redirect URI and installation are allow-listed exactly with no host normalization; every capability and scope decision comes from the trusted Zuri identity resolver, without a platform grant. All four responses are `Cache-Control: no-store`, and no error echoes a raw code, token or internal message. Authorization now requires consent that was actually given: `GET /authorize` renders a screen and mints nothing, and minting requires a POST carrying all three of the session cookie, a session-bound anti-CSRF token and an HMAC-signed request token that is the sole source of the parameters acted on, with a 5-minute TTL and every field re-validated against live configuration on approval. The anti-CSRF token is redundant with `SameSite=Lax` today and is kept so the defence does not live entirely in a cookie attribute another change could remove silently. Refusal is available and answered as `error=access_denied` with the original `state`. Expired codes and sessions have a locally tested maintenance service that retains each code while an unrevoked, unexpired linked session can still be revoked on replay. Production maintenance invocation, client registration, device-binding evidence and security sign-off remain outstanding. | `app/(entry)/plugin/authorize/page.jsx`, `app/api/plugin/auth/authorize/route.js`, `app/api/plugin/auth/capabilities/route.js`, `app/api/plugin/auth/revoke/route.js`, `app/api/plugin/auth/token/route.js`, `modules/identity/plugin-auth-service.js`, `modules/identity/plugin-consent-access.js`, `modules/identity/plugin-consent-view.js`, `modules/identity/plugin-consent.js` | `e2e/fr123-plugin-consent.spec.js`, `integration/fr123-plugin-auth-reaper.test.js`, `integration/fr123-plugin-consent-gate.test.js`, `unit/fr123-plugin-auth-migration.test.js`, `unit/fr123-plugin-auth-route.test.js`, `unit/fr123-plugin-auth-service.test.js`, `unit/fr123-plugin-consent-view.test.js`, `unit/fr123-plugin-consent.test.js` | ✅ |

| SEC-026 | Erasure is downstream of offboarding and never a substitute for it: `erasePrincipal` refuses 409 PRINCIPAL_HAS_LIVE_GRANTS while the principal holds any non-revoked Membership, RoleBinding or PlatformGrant in the tenant being erased, and where it does proceed it deletes `PersonCredential` and sets `Person.accessDisabledAt`, because a redacted person whose password still works is not erased. Erasure never revokes grants itself — that would make a data-subject request an administrative act with no named author and bury the moment authority ended inside a deliberately redacted trail. | `app/api/platform/users/offboard/route.js`, `modules/identity/membership-lifecycle-service.js` | `integration/fr191-access-grant-lifecycle.test.js`, `unit/membership-lifecycle-service.test.js` | ✅ |
| SEC-027 | Installation-operator access is time-boxed and its use is auditable, not only its grant. Every non-bootstrap `PlatformGrant` carries a mandatory `expiresAt` capped at 90 days, honoured by `hasOperatorGrant` even while the row is still nominally ACTIVE; renewal is a fresh row so the history of who held operator access, and when, is never overwritten. Reading the installation-wide audit stream and previewing or restoring a whole-installation backup are support-class reads (ADR-017 D6) and each records one `OPERATOR_ACTION` audit event on use — a denied attempt records nothing. | `modules/commerce/application/payment-service.js` | `integration/backup.test.js`, `integration/fr163-payment.test.js`, `integration/fr196-segregation-of-duties.test.js`, `integration/fr196-sod-cannot-be-assembled.test.js` | ✅ |

| NFR-009 | Local file operations are crash-recoverable and portable: authoritative metadata survives restart/remount, cache is fully rebuildable, absolute device paths never become identity, and canonical results are available when cache is stale or absent. | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
| NFR-010 | LINE business answers fail closed within a bounded request deadline: network calls use explicit timeouts/cancellation, duplicate delivery is idempotent, provider failure cannot expose secrets or raw data, and offline/local evaluation remains possible through an injected adapter. | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
| NFR-011 | Production tenancy changes are migration-first, idempotent and reversible: advisory/lock timeouts bound deployment, reserved code/UUID collisions abort, schema history and backups are inspected before apply, and no runtime secret or service-role key is stored in source, migration SQL, logs or browser code. | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
| NFR-012 | Activation evidence is deterministic, versioned and redacted: credentials come only from process environment/approved secret stores; reports contain hashes and assertion results rather than raw authorization material, database URLs, reply tokens or PII. | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
| NFR-013 | Binding activation and rollback are atomic, idempotent and bounded: exact row/version/evidence hashes are locked and compared, one correlation ID produces at most one state change, activation expires, and rollback disables routing before any secondary remediation. | `modules/agent/line-binding-activation.js`, `modules/agent/line-operator.js` | `integration/controlled-line-activation.postgres.test.js`, `integration/line-binding-activation.postgres.test.js`, `unit/activation-readiness-integration.test.js`, `unit/controlled-line-activation-migration.test.js`, `unit/fr055-postgres-target-guard.test.js`, `unit/line-binding-activation-cli.test.js`, `unit/line-binding-activation.test.js` | ✅ |
| NFR-014 | Authorization decisions are recomputed per turn and remain bounded, auditable and fail-closed across restart, horizontal instances, identity revocation and Membership revocation. | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
| NFR-015 | Phase 1 runtime source and secret resolution are explicit, asynchronous, bounded and redacted: production accepts only the Supabase Vault-backed SecretManagerPort, never constructs the local file vault or consumes raw model credentials; selection is binding-scoped, cache entries are versioned and scoped by Tenant/Business with hard expiry, rotation/revocation purges immediately, and provider failures never trigger implicit fallback. | `platform/integrations/core/secret-manager.js` | `unit/fr079-runtime-cutover.test.js` | ✅ |
| NFR-016 | Platform Integration management is bounded and secret-safe: UI/API actions use trusted Business scope, explicit async loading/error states, idempotency/version checks and redacted responses; raw secret material never crosses browser responses, Prisma, logs or audit events, and manager-unavailable states cannot report success. | — | — | 🔴 no anchor |
| NFR-017 | The LINE ingress is traceable end to end from one correlation id: every batch carries a correlation id — accepted from the trusted transport owner when it presents a well-formed one, generated otherwise — that appears on every structured record the request emits, on the HTTP response, and on the durable audit row for the message it produced, so an operator can join `webhook → identity → conversation → message` without archaeology. Records are emitted through one allowlisted emitter: message text, display names, bearer tokens, binding ids and reply tokens can never reach a log line, and a field the allowlist does not know is dropped and named rather than printed. Every rejected batch and every failed event emits a record naming the stage that failed; no failure is silent. | `app/api/agent/line-delivery/route.js`, `app/api/agent/line-webhook/route.js`, `lib/observability/correlation.js`, `lib/observability/logger.js`, `modules/crm/line-ingest-service.js` | `integration/agent-webhook-route.test.js`, `integration/line-account-isolation.test.js`, `integration/line-ingest.test.js`, `integration/line-oa-correlation-trace.test.js`, `integration/line-oa-cross-repo-round-trip.test.js`, `integration/line-oa-evidence-convergence.test.js`, `integration/line-reply-record.test.js`, `unit/observability-correlation.test.js`, `unit/observability-logger.test.js` | ✅ |
| NFR-018 | Market translation is deterministic and replay-safe: the same immutable raw evidence, payload hash, translation schema and observation type resolve to one logical observation, including concurrent workers through an atomic unique-lineage persistence boundary; translation failure leaves raw evidence unchanged and replayable. | `modules/knowledge/genesisrag17-lineage-repository.js`, `modules/market-intelligence/application/market-observation-service.js`, `modules/market-intelligence/application/translate-raw-record.js`, `modules/market-intelligence/infrastructure/market-observation-repository.js` | `integration/genesisrag17-tier1.test.js`, `unit/genesisrag17-lineage-repository.test.js`, `unit/market-intelligence-schema-migration.test.js`, `unit/market-intelligence/market-observation-repository.test.js`, `unit/market-intelligence/market-observation-service.test.js`, `unit/market-intelligence/translate-raw-record.test.js` | ✅ |

| NFR-020 | Every knowledge ingestion stage emits per-stage and pipeline-level metrics: each stage reports `records_in`, `records_out`, `records_failed`, `records_quarantined`, `processing_time` and `retry_count`; the pipeline reports `ingestion_lag`, `pipeline_latency`, `publication_latency`, `error_rate`, `quarantine_rate`, `entity_resolution_rate` and `retrieval_quality_score` (spec §32).
